# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_14465369.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd 
Subject: Building Fee Exception Expansion Ordinance 
Date: 
August 28, 2023 
 
Affordable Housing Task Force Recommendation:  
The fourth Affordable Housing Task Force recommendation from 2022 was that the City create a fee 
waiver for building permits for projects producing affordable housing. The recommendation was part of 
the Affordable Housing Task Force Work Session and Staff was directed to generally work on all items 
presented. The recommendation is attached. 
 
Background:  
The current City code has a section which gives building permits in the downtown area an exception to 
building permit fees. This code section includes a sunset date and sets up the program as more of a re-
imbursement than a waiver. As opposed to creating a new section of code, Staff proposes fulfilling the 
Affordable Housing Task Force’s recommendation by amending the current section. 
 
The proposed code amendment has the following features: 
• 
The inclusion of a fee exception for building permits for the creation of income restricted 
housing (per the recommendation of the Affordable Housing Task Force) 
• 
The inclusion of a fee exception for building permits for the maintenance of historic housing 
(requested as part of the change to this section of code) 
• 
Defining the limit of fees eligible to be reimbursed for the new exception types 
• 
Increasing the limit of fees eligible to be reimbursed for all exception types  
• 
Extending the sunset of the program generally 
The monetary limits were set based on an approximation based on the original downtown program. 
 
Although the Ordinance is proposing modifying the text of the existing section of code, a redline 
ordinance was deemed less clear than cleanly replacing the section. The redline is also attached for 
reference. 
 
The item for consideration is an amendment to City Code. This is approved by Ordinance. The Governing 
Body review schedule is anticipated as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
8/28/2023 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
9/5/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
9/11/2023 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
9/18/2023 
• 
Third and final reading  
9/25/2023 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Redline of existing code 
3. Affordable Housing Task Force Recommendation #4 

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 15.08.031, BUILDING PERMIT 
FEES–EXCEPTIONS 
FOR 
INCENTIVIZATION 
PROGRAM, 
OF 
CHAPTER 15.08, INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE ADOPTED, OF 
TITLE 15, BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO CREATE INCENTIVES FOR 
ATTAINABLE HOUSING AND HISTORIC PRESERVATION." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 15.08.031, Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization 
program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, of Title 15, Buildings and 
Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is amended to read: 
 
15.08.031 - Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization program. 
 
A. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found to successfully 
incentivize property development. The City of Cheyenne governing body desires to 
encourage and incentivize quality projects of the following varieties: 
 
1. Infill and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core; 
 
2. Residential development that contributes to attainable housing available to the 
workforce; and 
 
3. The rehabilitation of historic structures in ways which maintain historic integrity. 
 
B. The "City of Cheyenne Development Incentivization Program" is hereby established. To 
be eligible for the program, an applicant must satisfy the following conditions: 
 
1. Upon a form to be provided, complete and submit an application to the city planning 
and development department;  
 
2. A building permit application must be received by the city building office prior to June 
30, 2030; and 
 
3. Meet the specific requirements of at least one (1) of the following incentivization 
categories: 
 
a. Be located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in Exhibit A (map) attached to Ordinance No. 4241, on file 
12:58 pm, Aug 18, 2023

with the office of the city clerk and also available through the planning and 
development department; 
 
b. Provide housing that is income-restricted to eighty percent (80%) of area median 
income (AMI) as defined by the U.S. department of housing and urban 
development (HUD). The income restriction required under this subparagraph shall 
be for a period of not less than fifteen (15) years; or 
 
c. Be a contributing historic structure in a historic district and submit a building permit 
application which only proposes changes consistent with maintaining historic 
integrity. 
 
C. The city shall reimburse the building permit fees as specified under Section 15.08.030 of 
this chapter, as provided under this subsection. Building permits issued the previous fiscal 
year shall not be eligible for reimbursements under this subsection if received more than 
seven (7) days after the close of the fiscal year. No reimbursement of a building permit fee 
shall be issued before the completion of the attendant project. Building permit fees shall be 
reimbursed as follows: 
 
1. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project located within the 
boundaries of the core development incentivization program project area as provided 
by subparagraph (B)(3)(a) of this section; 
 
2. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
single-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; 
 
3. Up to ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
multi-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; or 
 
4. Up to one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) per program project that rehabilitates a historic 
structure in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(c) of this section. 
 
D. Planning and development department staff shall provide reports on prior fiscal year 
program activity to the governing body not less than annually. The reports shall contain: 
 
1. The total number of projects utilizing the program; 
 
2. The total amount of fees waived; and 
 
3. The total valuation of projects incentivized by the program. 
 
E. The total amount of building permit fees reimbursed under this section shall be capped for 
each fiscal year in accordance with the limitations imposed under this section and as 
determined by the director of the planning and development department. Reimbursements 
shall be limited as follows:  
 

1. Not more than $200,000 for the entire program; 
 
2. Not more than $100,000 for downtown core projects; 
 
3. Not more than $150,000 for affordable housing projects; and 
 
4. Not more than $20,000 for historic rehabilitation projects.  
 
F. A decision from the director of the planning and development department, or their 
designee, on any matters of interpretation in this section shall be final. 
 
G. This section is repealed effective July 1, 2030. 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 15.08.031, BUILDING PERMIT 
FEES–EXCEPTIONS 
FOR 
INCENTIVIZATION 
PROGRAM, 
OF 
CHAPTER 15.08, INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE ADOPTED, OF 
TITLE 15, BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO CREATE INCENTIVES FOR 
ATTAINABLE HOUSING AND HISTORIC PRESERVATION." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 15.08.031, Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization 
program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, of Title 15, Buildings and 
Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is amended to read: 
 
15.08.031 - Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization program. 
 
A. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found to successfully 
incentivize property development. The city City of Cheyenne governing body along with 
the downtown development authority desires to encourage and incentivize quality infill 
projects and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found 
to successfully incentivize property development. of the following varieties: 
 
1. Infill and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core; 
 
2. Residential development that contributes to attainable housing available to the 
workforce; and 
 
3. The rehabilitation of historic structures in ways which maintain historic integrity. 
 
B. A program, to be called the The "City of Cheyenne Core Development Incentivization 
Program," is hereby established. To be eligible for the program, an applicant must satisfy 
the following conditions: 
 
1. To be eligible for the program, an applicant must fulfill the following: 
 
a. 1. Upon a form to be provided, complete and submit an application to the city planning 
and development department;.  
 
b. 2. A building permit application must be received by the city building department 
office prior to June 30, 2025. 2030; and 
 

3. Meet the specific requirements of at least one (1) of the following incentivization 
categories: 
 
a. Be located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in Exhibit A (map) attached to Ordinance No. 4241, on file 
with the office of the city clerk and also available through the planning and 
development department; 
 
b. Provide housing that is income-restricted to eighty percent (80%) of area median 
income (AMI) as defined by the U.S. department of housing and urban 
development (HUD). The income restriction required under this subparagraph shall 
be for a period of not less than fifteen (15) years; or 
 
c. Be a contributing historic structure in a historic district and submit a building permit 
application which only proposes changes consistent with maintaining historic 
integrity. 
 
2. Reimbursements for building permits issued the previous fiscal year shall not be 
eligible if received more than seven days after the close of the fiscal year. 
 
C. The city shall waive reimburse the building permit fees, as set forth in specified under 
Section 15.08.030 of this chapter, up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program 
project located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in exhibit A (map) attached to ordinance no. 4241. A map of the 
program project area is also available through the planning and development department. 
as provided under this subsection. Building permits issued the previous fiscal year shall 
not be eligible for reimbursements under this subsection if received more than seven (7) 
days after the close of the fiscal year. No reimbursement of a building permit fee shall be 
issued before the completion of the attendant project. Building permit fees shall be 
reimbursed as follows: 
 
1. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project located within the 
boundaries of the core development incentivization program project area as provided 
by subparagraph (B)(3)(a) of this section; 
 
2. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
single-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; 
 
3. Up to ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
multi-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; or 
 
4. Up to one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) per program project that rehabilitates a historic 
structure in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(c) of this section. 
 
D. A decision from the director of planning and development, or their designee, on any matters 
of interpretation in this section shall be final. 

 
E. D. 
Planning and development department staff shall provide reports on prior fiscal 
year program activity to the governing body not less than annually. The reports shall 
contain: 
 
1. The total number of projects utilizing the program;. 
 
2. The total amount of fees waived;. and 
 
3. The total valuation of projects incentivized by the program. 
 
F. E. 
The total amount of building permit fees waived reimbursed under this section shall 
be capped at one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000.00) for each fiscal year in accordance 
with the limitations imposed under this section and as determined by the director of the 
planning and development director. department. Reimbursements shall be limited as 
follows:  
 
1. Not more than $200,000 for the entire program; 
 
2. Not more than $100,000 for downtown core projects; 
 
3. Not more than $150,000 for affordable housing projects; and 
 
4. Not more than $20,000 for historic rehabilitation projects.  
 
F. A decision from the director of the planning and development department, or their 
designee, on any matters of interpretation in this section shall be final. 
 
G. The provisions of Section 15.08.031 of this chapter shall be in effect until June 30, 2025. 
This section is repealed effective July 1, 2030. 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

Honorable Mayor Collins and Esteemed Cheyenne City Council 
2101 O’Neil Ave. 
Cheyenne, WY  82001 
Recommendation Number Four, Fee Waiver Program 
Dear Mayor Collins and Members of City Council, 
We the members of the Affordable Housing Task Force recommend the Mayor’s Office and City 
Council/City Staff immediately start creating a Fee Waiver/Reimbursement Program for   
developers/builders to incentivize the building of affordable housing. The program would waive the 
impact and/or building permit fee for affordable housing developments that are income restricted to 
those who earn at or below 80% of AMI.  The waiver/reimbursement program will also be available for 
those seeking to build shelters or other projects that benefit people experiencing homelessness.  
We anticipate this program being very similar to the fee waiver/reimbursement program that is 
currently in place for downtown development. The Fee Waiver application would be due at the time of 
submission of the development to the planning commission for their review and verification that the 
development will be income restricted.  
 
Respectfully, 
 
Brenda L. Birkle, Chair 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_36988238.pdf
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
August 7, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Re-Appropriation to be considered for approval 
 
 
On August 14, 2023, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
to re-appropriate funds for the Fiscal Year (FY) 2023 City of Cheyenne Budget per W.S. § 16-4-
112 and 16-4-113.  More information is detailed below regarding this final FY 2023 re-
appropriation request.  
 
GENERAL FUND RESERVES REQUEST 
The following General Fund reserve requests are included for your consideration: 
City Council Health Insurance Coverage Changes   
Members of the City Council made changes to their health insurance coverages during last fiscal 
year that were not included in the budget.  I am asking for $20,745 from reserves to pay for the 
increase to this line item. 
Vacation Accrual Payouts for Terminated Employees 
During FY 2023, the City has paid out several sick and vacation accrual leave balances once an 
employee terminated their employment.  Generally, accrual payouts are not budgeted for unless a 
retirement is anticipated.  If the position is replaced immediately, the budget is not enough to cover 
the accrual payout.  I am requesting a total increase of $57,572 from reserves to cover these 
expenses. 
Increase in Natural Gas and Electricity and Fleet Fuel, Labor, and Parts Costs   
My department estimates the natural gas and electricity costs for city buildings when the budget is 
developed each year.   In December 2022 and January 2023, extremely frigid temperatures across 
the nation forced homes and businesses to increase their heat during those two months, which sent 
the price of natural gas soaring.  The average spot price for natural gas at the Opal Hub surpassed 
$26 per thousand cubic feet for more than a month last winter.  Normal levels are $3.98 per 
thousand cubic feet.  As a result, the City’s projections for natural gas costs were inadequate.  
Therefore, I am requesting $93,700 from General Fund reserves to cover this shortage.   

Additionally, the City’s expenditures for fuel, labor and parts have exceeded the approved FY 
2023 budget for several General Fund departments.  I am requesting an increase of $131,323 from 
General Fund reserves to cover this budget shortfall. 
Vandalism Expenses    
Throughout the fiscal year, the Facilities Maintenance, Forestry, Aquatics, Golf Courses, Parks, 
Cemetery, and Clean and Safe Divisions have been forced to pay for unforeseen vandalism 
expenses to City assets.   These expenses were not budgeted for, as it is difficult to estimate 
these types of costs when the budget is initially prepared.  I am requesting an increase of 
$45,639 from reserves to cover these costs. 
Insurance Claims 
Repair costs due to accidents are unpredictable; therefore, when the budget is initially prepared 
each year, I do not forecast the City’s portion of loss claims. Instead, I choose to handle these 
expenditures through a budget reappropriation at the end of the fiscal year.   
I am requesting increases for the following departments:   

Police Department - $25,433

Fire Department - $6,559, and

Community Recreation and Events - $5,000
 Information on each incident with a cost over $1,000 are included below:  
Police Department

Summary of Claim #4183CH:  An employee was conducting a traffic stop and requested
additional backup.  Two more employees responded and parked behind the employee
conducting the traffic stop.  A driver under the influence drove into the back of the rear
vehicle, which pushed that vehicle into the other vehicles parked in front of it.  A claim has
been filed with the Wyoming Association of Risk Management (WARM).  WARM is
working with the District Attorney’s office on restitution.  The cost to the City is the $5,000
deductible.

Summary of Claim #4289CH:  An employee came to a brief stop and proceeded to enter
the intersection.  They did not see a vehicle traveling through the intersection and struck
the vehicle.  The cost to the City is the $5,000 deductible.

Summary of Claim #4414CH:  An employee was backing out of their driveway to leave
for work and hit a fence post.  The repairs were $3,543.98, which is under the deductible
limit.

Summary of Claim  #4388CH:  An employee was driving their vehicle out of the Public
Safety Center’s basement parking and struck the side of the wall. The repairs were
$3,658.65, which is under the deductible limit.


Summary of Claim  #4317CH:  An employee was traveling on Dell Range and proceeded
through a green light.  A citizen turned onto Dell Range into the lane the employee was
traveling in and caused the accident.  The insurance company’s estimate did not match the
cost of repairs.  WARM contacted citizen’s the insurance company but they would not
change their estimate.  The amount not covered by the citizen’s insurance was $1,118.65.

Summary of Claim  #4227CH:  An employee was responding to an accident using their
lights and siren on Dell Range.  The employee cleared the intersection and proceeded
through.  A citizen struck the employee’s vehicle.  A property claim was filed with WARM
and the cost to the City was the $5,000 is deductible.
Fire Department 
•
Summary of Claim #3812CH:  The employee was leaving the bay with a ladder truck and 
did not know the door was closing. The cost to the City is the $5,000 deductible.
•
Summary of Claim #4194CH:  An employee hit a citizen’s vehicle in an intersection. Both 
parties claimed they did not see the other and the employee was given a citation which was 
later dismissed.  The citizen’s insurance filed a claim and WARM denied it.  WARM filed 
a claim for damages with the citizen’s insurance company and was denied.  WARM 
then received a partial payment from the citizen’s insurance company.  The repairs not 
covered were $3,533.09.
Community Recreation and Events Department 

Summary of Claim #4308CH:  An aquatics employee was in the middle of an intersection
when a citizen’s vehicle ran the stop sign and collided with the city vehicle.  The vehicle
responsible for the collision left the scene.  Witnesses attempted to follow the vehicle but
were not able to stay with it.  The city vehicle was totaled, and a property claim was filed
with WARM.  The total claim was $43,973 and the City was reimbursed for everything but
the $5,000 deductible.
Fire Department Overtime 
The Fire Department responded to several major incidents which created increased overtime costs. 
These major incidents include fires at 2004 E. Lincolnway, 115 and 117 Longs Peak Drive and 
1500 W. College Drive as well as the gas leak at the Granite Rehabilitation Wellness Center.  There 
were also three extended sick leaves for occupational cancer, PTSD and COVID exposure.  I am 
requesting $75,000 to cover the higher overtime costs.   

OTHER FUNDS – NEW REVENUE AND RESERVES REQUESTS 
Weed and Pest Fund 

An increase of $80,000 is requested for revenue received from the Laramie County Weed
and Pest to help pay for the Crow Creek goat grazing project.
Recreation Programs Fund 

An increase of $68,745 is requested for sponsorships received to pay for expenditures for
Fridays on the Plaza.

An increase of $16,266 in revenue is requested to pay for child care program expenditures.

A transfer of $8,385 from the Superday reserves account is requested to pay for
expenditures related to this year’s Superday celebration.

An increase of $17,703 in concessions revenue is requested to pay for inventory.

An increase of $3,440 in Botanic Gardens revenue is requested to pay for gift shop
inventory expenses.
One Percent Sales Tax Fund 

A transfer of $501,881 from this fund’s Fire Department reserves account is requested to
pay for increased fire department maintenance and equipment expenditures.
Self Insurance Fund 

An increase of $1,174,600 in stop loss claim revenue is requested to pay for claims that
have been incurred but not reported (IBNR) as of June 30, 2023, and other expenditures.
Miscellaneous Agency Fund 
•
An increase of $196,572 in property and liability insurance claims revenue is requested to 
pay for city insurance claim expenditures.
•
An increase of $120 in the car seat program revenue account is requested to pay for 
expenditures pertaining to the Fire Department’s car seat program.
•
An increase of $16,901 in greenway projects revenue is requested to pay for increased 
greenway expenditures.
Perpetual Care Fund 

An increase of $8,818 in the investment interest revenue account is requested.  When a
customer purchases a cemetery lot, $400 of the fee goes into the fund.  At the end of each
month, my department computes the amount of interest earned on the investments in this
fund and then transfers this interest to the General Fund.  The $400 per lot perpetual care
fees is never transferred to the General Fund.
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_84453295.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
August 7, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Creation of an Urban Renewal Authority Fiduciary Fund 
On August 14, 2023, the Governing Body will be referring to the Finance Committee a 
resolution that establishes an Urban Renewal Authority Fiduciary Fund.    
In July, the City received its first Tax Increment Financing (TIF) payment from the Laramie 
County Treasurer in the amount of $12,656.69 for property taxes collected in FY 2023.   The 
payment includes the following Urban Renewal Plan and Projects: 
•
$7,745.70 – 15th Street
•
$1,194.01 – Hynds and the Hole
•
$3,716.98 – Hitching Post
These payments were received for property taxes over the frozen base amount for all properties 
within the urban renewal plan areas.  As these funds do not belong to the City, a fiduciary fund 
must be established.  A fiduciary fund is used in governmental accounting to segregate 
assets that must be held in trust for others. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email me at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR THE ESTABLISHMENT OF AN URBAN RENEWAL 
AUTHORITY FIDUCIARY FUND." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne established Urban Renewal 
Authority by Ordinance No. 4381 as an economic development tool for the purpose of revitalizing 
deteriorating or underdeveloped areas in the community; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body declared portions of the municipality as blighted areas 
through Resolution No. 6156 as required by W.S. § 15-9-106; 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to W.S. § 15-9-110, the Governing Body may approve urban 
renewal plans for the purpose of eliminating blight and preventing the spread of slum or blighted 
areas through redevelopment; 
 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with W.S. § 15-9-120, an urban renewal plan may contain a 
provision that taxes, if any, levied upon taxable property in an urban renewal project each year 
may be frozen at a base amount on the last date of equalization for all properties within the urban 
renewal plan area; 
 
WHEREAS, as property values increase, all new tax revenue above the frozen base amount 
are to be apportioned to the Urban Renewal Authority; 
 
WHEREAS, in governmental accounting, fiduciary funds are used to account for resources 
held by an agency as a trustee or in a custodial capacity for outside parties and these resources 
cannot be used to support the agency’s own programs; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governmental Accounting Standards Board (GASB) Statement No. 84 
establishes criteria for fiduciary activities of all state and local governments for purposes of 
accounting and financial reporting; and 
 
WHEREAS, an Urban Renewal Authority Fiduciary Fund is necessary to hold and disburse 
the levied taxes collected in excess of the frozen amount as approved by the Urban Renewal 
Authority Board for each urban renewal project. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that an Urban Renewal Authority Fiduciary Fund be established 
effective June 30, 2023, and that all portions of the levied taxes received for each urban renewal 
plan be placed in this fund. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City Treasurer be directed to coordinate with the 
Planning and Development Department to oversee the management and disbursement of the Urban 
Renewal Authority Fiduciary Fund for the period of time specified for each urban renewal plan. 
11:46 am, Jul 31 2023

 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_14422527.pdf
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE ACCEPTANCE OF A FEDERAL 
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT AGENCY FY 2022 ASSISTANCE TO 
FIREFIGHTERS GRANT IN THE AMOUNT OF $387,043.63 AND 
AUTHORIZING THE CONTRIBUTION OF $38,704.37 FROM THE FIFTH 
AND SIXTH PENNY FUNDS IN SUPPORT OF THIS PROGRAM, FOR 
THE 
PURPOSE 
OF 
PURCHASING 
BREATHABLE 
AIR 
FILL 
STATIONS." 
 
WHEREAS, the Fiscal Year (FY) 2022 Assistance to Firefighters Grant ("AFG") is a grant 
program from the Department of Homeland Security ("DHS") and the Federal Emergency 
Management Agency ("FEMA"). The primary goal of the AFG grant is to meet the firefighting 
and emergency response needs of fire departments and non-affiliated emergency medical service 
organizations; 
 
WHEREAS, AFG has helped firefighters and other first responders obtain critically needed 
resources necessary for protecting the public and emergency personnel from fire and related 
hazards.  Since 2001, AFG has helped firefighters and other first responders obtain critically 
needed equipment, protective gear, emergency vehicles, training and other resources necessary for 
protecting the public and emergency personnel from fire and related hazards; 
 
WHEREAS, the AFG Program requires that certain criteria is met as described in the AFG 
application guidance materials the City of Cheyenne’s application met that criteria; 
 
WHEREAS, On August 11, 2023, FEMA awarded the City of Cheyenne three hundred 
eighty-seven thousand forty-three dollars and sixty-three cents ($387,043.63) through its safety 
and operations program; and, 
 
WHEREAS, as a condition of this grant, the City is required to contribute non-federal funds 
equal to or greater than ten percent (10%) of the federal funds awarded, or thirty-eight thousand 
seven hundred four dollars and thirty-seven cents ($38,704.37) for a total approved budget of four 
hundred twenty-five thousand seven hundred and forty-eight dollars ($425,748.00). 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body approve the receipt of 
three hundred eighty-seven thousand forty-three dollars and sixty-three cents ($387,043.63) in 
federal funds for the AFG program and authorizes the contribution of ten percent (10%) of the 
federal funds awarded, or thirty-eight thousand seven hundred four dollars and thirty-seven cents 
($38,704.37), from fifth and sixth penny funds in support of this program for the purpose of 
purchasing breathable air fill stations. 
 
 
 
 
4:39 pm, Aug 24 2023

 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_68994353.pdf
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
Subject: A resolution supporting the conversion of a portion of Dutcher Ball Fields, located at 4200 
Converse Avenue from perpetual public outdoor recreation use and committing to 
purchase and encumber one additional replacement property to be deed restricted for 
perpetual outdoor recreation use pursuant to the requirements set forth in the Land and 
Water Conservation Fund Act. 
 
Date: 
August 23, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution of support required by the LWCF conversion process to request 
conversion of a portion of Dutcher Ball Fields (Saddle Tramps Site), approximately 2.5 acres, to be 
removed from perpetual public outdoor recreation use for a new city fire station.  The LWCF regulations 
require that we provide other suitable properties that meet LWCF requirements such as being of at least 
equal fair market value and of reasonably equivalent usefulness as trade.  In October of 2022, the 
Governing Body, with Resolution #6278, previously approved three properties as replacement properties 
with all three having a total value of $537,000.00.  The value did not meet the requirement of at least 
equal fair market value as compared to the Dutcher Ball Field (Saddle Tramps) Fire Station site value of 
$540,000.00, therefore, an additional property is needed for conversion. Appraisal has been completed 
for the Lewark property and along with the previous 3 properties meets the requirement of equal fair 
market value.  The Lewark property is intended to be purchased with 6th Penny Greenway Expansion 
funding and will provide needed land for the Sun Valley Open Space to Kiwanis Park Greenway Connector.  
 
Upon purchase, the City will be required to place deed restrictions on all four replacements properties 
which will restrict their use to outdoor recreational uses in perpetuity. 
 
Enclosures: 
Resolution 
Map 
Deed to include restriction 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CONVERSION OF A PORTION 
OF DUTCHER BALL FIELDS LOCATED AT 4200 CONVERSE AVENUE 
FROM PERPETUAL PUBLIC OUTDOOR RECREATION USE AND 
COMMITING TO PURCHASE AND ENCUMBER ONE ADDITIONAL 
REPLACEMENT PROPERTY TO BE DEED RESTRICTED FOR 
PERPETUAL PUBLIC OUTDOOR RECREATION USE PURSUANT TO 
THE REQUIREMENTS SET FORTH IN THE LAND AND WATER 
CONSERVATION FUND ACT." 
 
WHEREAS, The City desires to convert approximately two point five (2.5) acres of the 
Dutcher Ball Field ("Saddle Tramps") property to a non-recreational use to build a municipal fire 
station; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 6(f)(3) of the Land and Water Conservation Fund Act ("LWCF") 
(now codified at 54 U.S.C. § 200305(f)(3)) and its implementing regulations at 36 C.F.R. Part 59 
requires that no property acquired or developed with LWCF assistance shall be converted to other 
than public outdoor recreation uses without the approval of the Secretary of the Interior, and only 
if the Secretary finds it to be in accordance with the then existing Statewide Comprehensive 
Outdoor Recreation Plans ("SCORP") and only upon such conditions as the Secretary deems 
necessary to assure the substitution of other recreation properties of at least equal fair market value 
and of reasonably equivalent usefulness and location. Parks or recreation areas acquired or 
developed with LWCF assistance (in whole or part) are now referred to as "LWCF-assisted areas" 
and/or "LWCF boundary areas."; 
 
WHEREAS, the City has agreed previously and pursuant to Resolution #6278 to take 
actions to purchase three (3) properties as replacement and has agreed to allow deed restriction for 
perpetual outdoor recreation use; 
 
WHEREAS, the City intends to take action to purchase an additional property as 
replacement and agrees to allow deed restriction for perpetual outdoor recreation use (Greenway 
corridor) as described in attached exhibit, at the time of purchase; 
 
WHEREAS, the proposed additional replacement property description is as follows and as 
described in attached exhibit; and, 
 
Property #4, 4.36 acres, legal description:  
 
A tract of Land situated in the SE¼SE¼ and the SW¼SE¼ of Section 35, Township 
14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, more 
particularly described as follows: 
 
Commencing at the southwest corner of said Section 35; thence 77°50'00", grid 
azimuth from North, City of Cheyenne Datum, a distance of 2406.81 feet to the 
7:02 pm, Aug 17 2023

point of beginning, said point also being a point on the South line of Sun Valley 
Addition Fourteenth Filing; thence 55°40'30", along the southerly line of said Sun 
Valley Addition Fourteenth Filing, a distance of 457.14 feet; thence 145°32'22", a 
distance of 129.07 feet; 51°52'57", a distance of 567.85 feet; thence 325°40'30", a 
distance of 91.51 feet, to a point on the South line of said Sun Valley Addition 
Fourteenth Filing; thence 55°40'30", along said South line, a distance of 558.51 
feet, to the West line of Chukker Ridge 2nd Filing; thence 145°33'02", along said 
West line, a distance of 160.26 feet; thence 235°43'23", a distance of 1582.13 feet; 
thence 325°23'33"; a distance of 158.94 feet to the point of beginning. Containing 
4.36 acres more or less. 
 
WHEREAS, the project meets the criteria for the Land and Water Conservation Fund 
Conversion; and, 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
supports a Land and Water Conservation Fund land conversion; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne or other City 
official designated by the Mayor, is authorized to sign any necessary applications and/or other 
documents as required for a Land and Water Conservation Fund land conversion; and,  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne is hereby 
designated as the authorized representative of the City to act on behalf of the Governing Body on 
all matters relating to this conversion application, and in the event the conversion is awarded to 
the City of Cheyenne for this project, the Mayor is authorized to execute any necessary agreements 
and associated documents. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_70368098.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Lonnie Olson– Planner I 
Subject: A Resolution Approving the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and the West Downtown 
Urban Renewal Project Pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-9-110. 
Date: 
August 28, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Public Hearing  
8/28/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
 
9/06/2023 
• 
Second and Final Reading  
 
9/11/2023 
 
Summary:  
 
The City of Cheyenne adopted Ordinance No. 4381 and Resolution No. 6156 on June 28, 2021 
authorizing the creation and use of powers for the Urban Renewal Authority. Included within the Urban 
Renewal Powers is the ability to make Urban Renewal Plans and Projects. The attached plan serves to 
establish the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project for the City of Cheyenne. The Governing 
Body adopted a Resolution on July 10, 2023 declaring the area as blight pursuant to § 15-9-107, thus 
making the area eligible for an Urban Renewal Plan and Project. The West Downtown Urban Renewal 
Plan will overview the urban renewal boundaries of the project area, the conditions of blight identified, 
all authorized urban renewal activities, the project financing (including tax increment financing), the 
terms of the plan, the conformance of the urban renewal project with adopted City of Cheyenne plans, 
the conformance of the project with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110, and the procedures to amend the plan. 
 
The Urban Renewal Authority Board heard the plan at their August 3, 2023 meeting and recommended 
approval by a vote of 4-0. The item was then heard at the August 21, 2023 Planning Commission 
meeting and was recommended approval by a vote of 4-0. The Planning Commission staff report is 
attached and unchanged, except that the plan and project is attached to the resolution instead of the 
staff report.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

1 
 
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE WEST DOWNTOWN URBAN 
RENEWAL PLAN AND THE WEST DOWNTOWN URBAN RENEWAL 
PROJECT PURSUANT TO WYOMING STATUTE § 15-9-110." 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 6334 and under Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-101, et seq., 
the City of Cheyenne found certain areas to be appropriate for an urban renewal project, including 
the "Expanded Downtown Study Area" as described in Resolution No. 6334; 
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 6334 was adopted after hearings on July 10, 2023 and was 
found to be in conformity and appropriate for an urban renewal project pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 
15-9-107; 
 
WHEREAS, on August 3, 2023 the City Urban Renewal Authority Board: (i) reviewed the 
West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan for the West Downtown Urban Renewal Project; (ii) found 
the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan to be in conformity with PlanCheyenne; and, (iii) 
recommended that the Planning Commission recommend approval of  the West Downtown Urban 
Renewal Plan for the West Downtown Urban Renewal Project in the form presented to the Urban 
Renewal Authority Board; 
 
WHEREAS, Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-108 requires that before approving an urban renewal 
project, the Governing Body shall have submitted the applicable urban renewal plan to the City 
Planning Commission, for review and recommendations as to its conformity with PlanCheyenne, 
which is the general plan for the development of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, on August 21, 2023, the City Planning Commission: (i) reviewed the West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Plan for the West Downtown Urban Renewal Project; (ii) found the 
West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan to be in conformity with PlanCheyenne; and, (iii) 
recommended that the Governing Body adopt the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan for the 
West Downtown Urban Renewal Project in the form presented to the Planning Commission; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body held a public hearing on the West Downtown Urban 
Renewal Plan and Project in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-109(a); 
 
WHEREAS, on August 12, 2023 and August 19, 2023, notice of the public hearing 
required by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-109(b) was published in a newspaper having a general circulation 
in the area; 
 
WHEREAS, on July 27, 2023, notice was mailed to all affected taxing agencies and 
property owners in the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project area; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan 
and the West Downtown Urban Renewal Project, which is attached hereto and incorporated herein 
6:14 pm, Aug 21 2023

2 
as Attachment A, and, for the reasons set forth in such plan is able to make, and hereby makes, the 
following findings: 
(i)
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan conforms to PlanCheyenne, and is
consistent with definite local objectives respecting infill opportunities, locations for
future revitalization, downtown revitalization and mix of uses, infill and
revitalization incentives, building reuse, mix of activities downtown, and
downtown placemaking; and,
(ii)
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan is sufficiently complete to indicate land
acquisition, demolition and removal of structures, redevelopment, improvements,
and rehabilitation as may be proposed to be carried out in the urban renewal area,
zoning and planning changes, if any, land used, maximum densities and building
requirements;
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan 
and the West Downtown Urban Renewal Project, and, for the reasons set forth in such plan is able 
to make, and hereby makes, the following findings: 
(i)
Because the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan does not propose relocating 
any residential properties, there is no need to designate a method for relocation 
of families displaced from the urban renewal area;
(ii)
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan conforms to PlanCheyenne as a whole;
(iii)
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan gives due consideration to the provision 
of adequate park and recreational areas and facilities that may be desirable for 
neighborhood improvement, with special consideration for the health, safety and 
welfare of children residing in the general vicinity of the site covered by the West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Plan; and,
(iv)
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan affords maximum opportunity, 
consistent with the municipality's needs, for the rehabilitation or redevelopment of 
the urban renewal area by private enterprise;
WHEREAS approval of the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project authorizes 
division of property taxes pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120 for the following properties: 
(i)
All of Blocks 332, 351, 352, 392, 393, 394, of Original City, Cheyenne,
Wyoming; and,
(ii)
All of Andiamo Subdivision, HGI Plaza, and Nishigawa Annex, Cheyenne,
Wyoming; and,
WHEREAS, division of property taxes shall be collected and deposited into the Urban 
Renewal Fiduciary Fund and segregated for the purpose of the Urban Renewal Authority. 

 
3 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
hereby approves the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and West Downtown Urban Renewal 
Project as shown in Attachment A.

West Downtown | Page 1
Introduction
The City of Cheyenne adopted Ordinance No. 
4381 and Resolution No. 6156 on June 28, 2021 
authorizing the creation and use of powers 
for the Urban Renewal Authority. This action 
fulfilled the requirements in Wyo. Stat. § 15-
9-106, § 15-9-107, § 15-9-133, and § 15-9-134 
as the Governing Body adopted a resolution 
declaring one or more slum or blighted areas in 
the community and that the redevelopment of 
such areas are necessary for the health, safety, 
morals or welfare of the community; and the 
Governing Body delegated the authorities of 
Urban Renewal to the Cheyenne Urban Renewal 
Authority. By completing the previous actions 
and pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-106, the City 
of Cheyenne has completed the prerequisites to 
exercise the authority conferred by the Wyoming 
Urban Renewal Code. 
This plan serves to establish the West Downtown 
Urban Renewal Plan and Project for the City of 
Cheyenne. The West Downtown Urban Renewal 
Plan will overview the urban renewal boundaries 
of the project area, the conditions of blight 
identified in the project area, all authorized 
urban renewal activities, the project financing, 
the terms of the plan, the conformance of 
the urban renewal project with adopted City 
of Cheyenne plans, the conformance of the 
project with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110, and the 
procedures to amend the plan. The 15th Street 
Corridor Urban Renewal Plan will be referred to 
the Urban Renewal Authority and City Planning 
Commission for recommendations. The plan 
and recommendations will be introduced before 
the Governing Body for final consideration 
through Resolution. The creation of the plan and 
adoption thereof serves to enable and fulfill Wyo. 
Stat. § 15-9-107 through § 15-9-111, § 15-9-113, 
§ 15-9-115 through § 15-9-116, and § 15-9-119 
through § 15-9-132. 
The West Downtown redevelopment is proposed 
to include commercial, residential, and office uses; 
or any mix of uses proposed by the development 
community. The plan has been created in 
anticipation of development opportunities in 
the area, including a proposed Downtown Hotel 
and the Cheyenne Children’s Museum. The area 
offers prime land for redevelopment with a 
large amount of the included properties being 
comprised of surface parking. While the exact 
redevelopment is yet to be determined, the City 
of Cheyenne will utilize its Urban Renewal powers 
to aid the redevelopment with the remediation 
of 
environmental 
contaminants, 
building 
rehabilitation, targeted building demolition, and 
upgrades to necessary public improvements. 
The City plans to use tax increment financing 
(TIF), enabled by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120, to aid 
in the completion of these objectives. The use 
of these Urban Renewal powers and adoption 
of the plan will permit the redevelopment of the 
West Downtown through a robust public/private 
partnership and permit the utilization of Urban 
Renewal tools.

West Downtown | Page 2
Urban Renewal Boundaries
The West Downtown Renewal Plan encompasses an area roughly bound by Bent Avenue to the west, Pi­
oneer Avenue to the east, W. 19th Street to the north from Bent Avenue to O’ Neil Avenue, W. 18th Street 
to the north between O’ Neil Avenue and Thomes Avenue, W. 17th Street to the north between Thomes 
Avenue and Pioneer Avenue. The map of the Urban Renewal Area can be found below. 
The legal description for the project area includes:
•	
All of Blocks 332, 351, 352, 392, 393, 394, of Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming
•           All of Andiamo Subdivision, HGI Plaza, and Nishigawa Annex, Cheyenne, Wyoming
•	
All public rights-of-way adjacent to the blocks referenced herein.
W. Lincolnway
W. 18th Street
Thomes Avenue
Bent Avenue

West Downtown | Page 3
Conditions Survey
The Expanded Downtown study area is shown 
in Figure 2. The study area includes the blocks 
between Bent Avenue and House Avenue from 
the east to west and between 20th Street and 
the Union Pacific Railroad from the north to 
south, continuing along from House Avenue to 
Morrie Avenue from the east to the west and 
between the alley of 17th and Lincolnway to 
the Union Pacific Railroad from the north to the 
south and was adopted by the Governing Body 
on July 10, 2023. The study area included seven 
(7) of the nine (9) conditions of blight identified 
in the survey, demonstrating an area of blight in 
the community.  
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
Staff noted several deteriorating buildings in the 
Expanded Downtown area. Examples include 
819 E 15th Street and 607 E Lincolnway; though 
several other buildings appear to be deteriorating 
and may be more so on the interior. 
Indicators of deterioration witnessed include 
evidence of fire, boarded up buildings, and faulty 
building siding. The adjacent photos and those 
in Appendix B illustrate the study area meets this 
condition of blight.
2. Predominance of Defective or Inadequate 
Street Layout
The eastern portion of the Expanded Downtown 
study area contains large lots with few street 
connections. Staff noted that between House 
Avenue and Morrie Avenue and 15th Street and 
Linconlnway had poor connectivity in relation to 
the surrounding areas. Due to the faulty street 
network and the lack of adequate access, staff 
is satisfied this condition of blight is met.
3. Faulty Lot Layout in Relation to Size, 
Accessibility or Usefulness
The adjacent image in Figure 2 depicts the faulty 
lot layout in the study area. Many blocks contain 
platted alleys while others have been vacated. 
This leads to confusion and lack of usable area 
for development purposes. In the eastern portion 
of the study area,  lots have been divided poorly 
leaving certain lots unusable to be developed. 
Supplemental to this evidence of blight, many 
lots have not been platted since the Original 
City subdivision. Staff has difficulty interpreting 
the Original City subdivision and the legal 
descriptions are often a source of confusion. The 
inconsistency in alley vacation and age of the 
subdivision leads to faulty lot layout in relation 
to size, accessibility and usefulness. 
4. Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
The Expanded Downtown area contained litter 
and a lack of connections between Linconlnway 
and East 15th street, meeting unsanitary 
conditions and unsafe conditions. Furthermore, 
the prevalence of industrial businesses and the 
age of the buildings in the area allude to possible 
environmental contamination. The litter, lack of 
connections, and environmental contamination 
confirm the presence of unsanitary and unsafe 
conditions throughout the Expanded Downtown 
study area. 
5. Deterioration of Site or Other Improvements
The Expanded Downtown study area has 
deteriorated or missing curb, gutter, and 
sidewalk in various locations. The deteriorated 
site conditions have exacerbated the pedestrian 
connections in the Expanded Downton. The 
locations that lack curb, gutter, and sidewalk 
create 
potential 
pedestrian 
and 
vehicular 
conflicts. Due to the deteriorated and missing 
curb, gutter, and sidewalk, staff is satisfied this 
condition of blight is met.    

West Downtown | Page 4
6. Diversity of Ownership, Tax or Other Special 
Assessments
The Expanded Downtown study area contains 
a wide variety of ownership, making planning 
holistically difficult. A wide variety of ownership 
fosters many different perspectives and needs 
to be met. The corridor contains both public and 
private ownership, as well. It is also noteworthy 
that there are several owners that reside out of 
state. Staff noted that lots have been divided and 
sold through informal subdivision processes 
and that has created issues with ownership of 
parcels of land. Due to the variety of ownership 
and informal subdivision process, staff is 
satisfied this condition of blight is met.  
7. The Existence of Conditions which Endanger 
Life or Property by Fire and Other Causes
The parcel located at 701 E Lincolnway burned 
in a fire in 2022 and remains a vacant lot. The 
potential environmental contamination from 
previous uses and the age of the structures lead 
to the probable contamination in the study area 
yield another condition that may endanger life 
and property. The noted fire and environmental 
contamination fulfill this condition of blight.

West Downtown | Page 5
Figure 2: Expanded Downtown Study Area

West Downtown | Page 6
Authorized Urban Renewal Activities
The conditions identified in the plan and project area 
yield that the property is in need of rehabilitation 
and that such redevelopment is in the best interest 
of the public health, safety, morals and welfare of 
the community. The City of Cheyenne will use the 
Urban Renewal powers authorized in Wyo. Stat. § 
15-9-113 to: 
•	
Improve streets; including curb, gutter, and 
sidewalks;
•	
relocate utilities as necessary;
•	
restore and improve historic structures that 
have fallen into disrepair;
•	
remediate environmental contamination;
•	
facilitate economic development and site 
beautification.
Upon passage of the Resolution adopting the 
West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project 
area, all authorized Urban Renewal activities 
will be eligible for tax increment financing (TIF) 
assistance.
Tax Increment Financing Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120
Pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120, the West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Plan authorizes the use 
of tax increment financing for the Urban Renewal 
Project Area as identified in the Urban Renewal 
Boundaries. Upon passage of the Resolution for 
this Urban Renewal Plan, taxes levied upon taxable 
property in:
•	
All of Blocks 332, 351, 352, 392, 393, 394, of 
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and
•	
All of Andiamo Subdivision, HGI Plaza and 
Nishigawa Annex, Cheyenne, Wyoming
shall be divided as follows:  
a.	 That portion of the taxes which would be 
produced by the rate upon which the tax is levied 
each year by or for each of the taxing agencies 
upon the total sum of the assessed value of the 
taxable property in the urban renewal project 
as shown upon the assessment roll used in 
connection with the taxation of the property 
by the taxing agency, last equalized prior to 
the effective date of the urban renewal project 
shall be allocated to and, when collected, paid 
into the funds of the respective taxing agencies 
as taxes by or for those taxing agencies on all 
other property are paid; and
b.	 That portion of the levied taxes each year in 
excess of the amount specified in paragraph 
(a) of this section shall be allocated to and, 
when collected, paid into a special fund of 
the City of Cheyenne to pay the principal and 
interest on loaned money advanced to, or any 
indebtedness  (whether funded, refunded, 
assessed, or otherwise) incurred by the City or 
the Urban Renewal Authority, unless the total 
assessed valuation of the taxable property for 
the aforementioned properties exceeds the 
total assessed value of the taxable property 
in the project as shown by the last equalized 
assessment roll referred to in paragraph (a) of 
this section, all of the taxes levied and collected 
upon the taxable property in the urban renewal 
project shall be paid into the funds of the 
respective taxing agencies. When any loans, 
advances and indebtedness, if any, and interest 
have been paid in full, all monies thereafter 
received from taxes upon the taxable property 
in the urban renewal project shall be paid into 
the funds of the various taxing agencies as 
taxes on all other property are paid.
The tax increment will be used to fulfill the 
purposes of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-121, which reads:
In any urban renewal plan or in proceedings 
for the advance of monies or making of loans 
or the incurring of any indebtedness by the 
municipality or agency to finance or refinance 
in whole or in part the urban renewal project, 
the portion of the taxes specified in W.S. 15-9-
120(a)(ii) may be irrevocably pledged for the 
payment of the principal of and interest on 
those loans or advances or that indebtedness.
Again, the tax increment financing will be used 

West Downtown | Page 7
Plan Financing
As mentioned in the previous section, the West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project will 
utilize tax increment financing. The base of all 
the properties included in the plan boundaries 
is $143,227.65. Like other Urban Renewal Plans 
and Project in the City of Cheyenne each project 
will be required to perform individually, meaning 
the project will only be able to collect TIF from 
the increment it creates. Due to development 
uncertainties and the number of properties in 
the project area, the Planning and Development 
Department has only performed a projection for 
the proposed downtown hotel. The site of the 
proposed downtown hotel in 2022 had a property 
tax of $1,274.67. Based on information provided 
by the developer, the tax increment generation for 
the duration of the plan and project would come 
out to approximatly $1.68 million. Comparable 
properties used to base this projection was the 
Fairfield Inn Downtown, Tru by Hilton, Holiday Inn 
Express, Staybridge, and SpringHill Suites. 
to aide in the completion of the Urban Renewal 
activities specified in this plan; which include;
•	
Improve streets; including curb, gutter, and 
sidewalks;
•	
relocate utilities as necessary;
•	
restore and improve historic structures that 
have fallen into disrepair;
•	
remediate environmental contamination;
•	
facilitate economic development and site 
beautification.
The City will utilize the TIF to pay principal 
and interest on loans the City or a designated 
developer(s) receives for the purposes of carrying 
out the activities specified in this plan. 
Map 1- Area of proposed Downtown Hotel

West Downtown | Page 8
Conformance with the Comprehensive Plan
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and 
Project complies with PlanCheyenne very similarly 
to the Hynds and the Hole and 15th Street Corridor 
Urban Renewal Plans. This is due to the fact they 
offer incentives via the tax increment financing, 
allow for the revitalization of Historic properties, 
and contain infill development. The West Downtown 
Plan helps achieve PlanCheyenne Foundation 5 - 
Celebrating our Character and Varied Heritages. 
This foundation states, “As Cheyenne grows, we 
will celebrate and enhance our character and 
heritage by focusing on preserving our historic 
areas and Downtown.” One of the buildings in the 
plan area is an historic building in the Downtown 
District. The activities authorized under the Urban 
Renewal Plan will ensure the Historic atmosphere 
is preserved while catalyzing new development 
opportunities along the corridor. 
Complementary to furthering Foundation 5, the 
Urban Renewal Plan and Project also reinforces 
the same Principles and Policies in PlanCheyenne 
as the 15th Street Corridor and Hynds and the 
Hole Plan:
•	
Policy 1.4.A: Infill Opportunities
•	
Policy 1.4.B: Locations for Future Revitalization 
•	
Policy 1.4.C: Downtown Revitalization and Mix 
of Uses
•	
Policy 1.4.D: Infill and Revitalization Incentives 
•	
Policy 1.4.E: Building Reuse
•	
Policy 3.2.A: Mix of Activites Downtown
•	
Policy 3.2.C Downtown Placemaking 
The policies above show that the Urban Renewal 
Plan addresses Principle 1.4 and 3.2. These 
policies and Principles represent an instance in 
which the Urban Renewal Plan is in conformance 
with the comprehensive plan. PlanCheyenne 
contains several more policies and principles 
in which the Urban Renewal Plan and Project 
conform to. Through revitalization, building reuse, 
infill development, and Historic Preservation, 
this Urban Renewal Plan furthers the intent and 
objectives of PlanCheyenne. 

West Downtown | Page 9
Conformance with Wyo. Stat. §15-9-110
In order for an Urban Renewal Plan and Project to 
be approved the Governing Body must find that 
the plan meets the review criteria found in Wyo. 
Stat. § 15-9-110(a) and (b). Due to the fact that 
the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan does 
not consist of open space to be acquired by the 
municipality, the plan will not address the findings 
of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110(b). Pursuant to Wyo. Stat. 
§ 15-9-110(a), the plan achieves the following 
requirements:
(i)	 A feasible method exists for the relocation 
of families who will be displaced from the 
urban renewal area in decent, safe and sanitary 
dwelling accommodations within their means 
and without undue hardship to those families;
The plan and project area boundaries do 
encompass residential properties; but 
there is no intent to relocate or displace 
families located in this project area. The 
housing within this area is in new and 
great condition. There is hope that this 
plan and project will  create more housing 
opportunities through the redevelopment 
of surface parking lots. 
(ii)	The urban renewal plan conforms to the 
general plan of the municipality as a whole;
PlanCheyenne is the Comprehensive Plan 
for the City of Cheyenne. As discussed in the 
previous section, the West Downtown Urban 
Renewal Plan conforms to PlanCheyenne.
(iii)	The 
urban 
renewal 
plan 
gives 
due 
consideration to the provision of adequate park 
and recreational areas and facilities that may 
be desirable for neighborhood improvement, 
with special consideration for the health, safety 
and welfare of children residing in the general 
vicinity of the site covered by the plan;
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan 
gives due consideration to the provision 
of adequate parks and recreation areas 
as a park is located just north of the 
project boundaries. The project area 
includes a children’s museum that is under 
construction which would provide for 
recreational facilities. In addition, any multi-
family development that would occur must 
contain open space which should have a 
recreation purpose.  
(iv)	The urban renewal plan affords maximum 
opportunity, consistent with the municipality’s 
needs, for the rehabilitation or redevelopment 
of the urban renewal area by private enterprise.
The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan 
affords the maximum opportunity for 
redevelopment by private enterprise. The 
plan is in anticipation for a downtown hotel 
which would create opportunities for areas 
in the vicinity to redevelop. 

West Downtown | Page 10
Plan Term
The Term of the West Downtown Urban Renewal 
Plan is 25 years from the approval of the 
Resolution by the Governing Body. The Plan shall 
be effective pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-111(b). 
If the Governing Body deems that all projects 
have been accomplished and all debts incurred 
to finance those projects and all expenses of the 
Authority have been repaid, the Governing Body 
may declare this Plan fully implemented and the 
total tax collections derived from this Plan Area 
shall be paid into the funds of the appropriate 
taxing entity.
Amendment Procedures
This plan shall be amended pursuant to Wyo. Stat. 
§ 15-9-111(a). All modifications to this Plan will be 
referred from the Urban Renewal Authority Board 
to the City of Cheyenne Governing Body and shall 
be approved by Resolution.

Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and 
Project, as recommended by the Urban Renewal Authority, noting the request meets the review criteria 
of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110.  
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLLR-23-2 
 
 
August 21, 2023 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City of Cheyenne adopted Ordinance No. 4381 and Resolution No. 6156 on June 28, 2021 
authorizing the creation and use of powers for the Urban Renewal Authority. This action fulfilled the 
requirements in Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-106, § 15-9-107, § 15-9-133, and § 15-9-134 as the Governing Body 
adopted a resolution declaring one or more slum or blighted areas in the community and that the 
redevelopment of such areas are necessary for the health, safety, morals or welfare of the community; 
and the Governing Body delegated the authorities of Urban Renewal to the Cheyenne Urban Renewal 
Authority. By completing the previous actions and pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-106, the City of 
Cheyenne has completed the prerequisites to exercise the authority conferred by the Wyoming Urban 
Renewal Code. 
 
This plan serves to establish the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project for the City of 
Cheyenne. The Governing Body adopted a Resolution on July 10th, 2023 declaring the area as blight 
pursuant to § 15-9-107, thus making the area eligible for an Urban Renewal Plan and Project. The West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Plan will overview the urban renewal boundaries of the project area, the 
conditions of blight identified in the project area, all authorized urban renewal activities, the project 
financing, the terms of the plan, the conformance of the urban renewal project with adopted City of 
Cheyenne plans, the conformance of the project with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110, and the procedures to 
amend the plan. The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan will be referred to the Urban Renewal 
Authority and City Planning Commission for recommendations. The plan and recommendations will be 
introduced before the Governing Body for final consideration through Resolution. The creation of the 
plan and adoption thereof serves to enable and fulfill Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-107 through § 15-9-111, § 15-9-
113, § 15-9-115 through § 15-9-116, and § 15-9-119 through § 15-9-132. 
 
The West Downtown redevelopment is proposed to include commercial, residential, and office uses; or 
any mix of uses proposed by the development community. The plan had been created in anticipation of 
development opportunities in the area, including a proposed Downtown Hotel. The area offers prime 
land for redevelopment with a large amount of the included properties being comprised of surface 
parking. While the exact redevelopment is yet to be determined, the City of Cheyenne will utilize its 
Urban Renewal powers to aid the redevelopment with the remediation of environmental contaminants, 
building rehabilitation, and upgrades to necessary public improvements. The City plans to use tax 
increment financing (TIF), enabled by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120, to aid in the completion of these objectives. 
The use of these Urban Renewal powers and adoption of the plan will permit the redevelopment of the 
West Downtown through a robust public/private partnership and permit the utilization of Urban 
Renewal tools. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PLLR-23-2 
 
 
August 21, 2023 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
The Planning Commission’s role in adoption of the Urban Renewal Plan and Project is to review and 
provide a recommendation to the Governing Body as to whether or not to approve per Wyo. Stat. § 15-
9-108. The specific review criteria are listed in Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110 and is summarized in the attached 
West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project document.        
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this Urban Renewal Plan and Project meets the applicable review criteria which is 
outlined in the attached Urban Renewal Plan and Project document.   
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends the Planning Commission recommend approval of the West Downtown Urban 
Renewal Plan and Project amendment as recommended by the Urban Renewal Authority, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110.  
 
 
URBAN RENEWAL AUTHORITY RECOMMENDATION: 
 
The Urban Renewal Authority recommend approval of the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan 
and Project as presented by staff, noting the Plan and Project conforms with Wyo. Stat. § 
15-9-110, and authorized submission to the Planning Commission for review and recommendation 
to the Governing Body. 
 
 
ATTACHMENT: 
 
A. The West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and Project  
 
 

 
 
 
 
 
 
The West Downtown Plan and Project has been 
attached to the Resolution as Attachment A  


--- END OF FILE ---

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins, City of Cheyenne 
 
 
City Council Members 
 
FROM:  
Thomas D. Cobb, PE, City Engineer 
 
DATE:  
August 22, 2023 
 
SUBJECT: 
A RESOLUTION TO ACCEPT AND APPROVE THE FEDERALLY FUNDED DRY 
CREEK DRAINAGE MASTER PLAN UPDATE SUBMITTED BY GLM DESIGN GROUP   
 
 
Please find the attached the above referenced resolution for your approval and execution.  The 
agreement is summarized below for your information.   
 
Purpose of Project: 
the purpose of the plan is to update the 1988 Dry Creek Drainage Plan to reflect risk to life and 
property resulting from new developments with effect the drainage.  The goal of the plan is to 
gather information necessary to identify and prioritize drainage mitigation projects which 
minimize negative impacts of current drainage problems and to inform future decisions on 
proposed development which minimize risk to life and property from poor drainage.  More details 
on the purpose and need are available on the City Share Drive at: 
 
S:\Engineering Department\Dry Creek Master Plan Update 
 
 
Purpose of Resolution 
The purpose of the resolution is to provide proper closeout of the FEMA Grant ID: 18FEMA-CHE-
HM-4327AAB, Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan Update Project administered by the Wyoming Office 
of Homeland Security. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
A COMMUNITY OF CHOICE 
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

 
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A 
RESOLUTION 
TO 
ACCEPT 
AND 
APPROVE 
THE 
FEDERALLY FUNDED DRY CREEK DRAINAGE MASTER PLAN 
UPDATE SUBMITTED BY GLM DESIGN GROUP." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne received a grant from the Federal Emergency 
Management Agency (FEMA), which is administered by the Wyoming Office of Homeland 
Security, to update the 1988 Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan ("Plan"); 
 
WHEREAS, the purpose of the FEMA grant was to provide funds to the City to update 
the Plan to reflect potential risk to life and property resulting from new development projects, 
which can effect drainage. The goal of the updated Plan is to gather information necessary to 
identify and prioritize drainage mitigation projects which minimize negative impacts of current 
drainage problems and to inform future decisions on proposed development which minimize risk 
to life and property from poor drainage; 
 
WHEREAS, over the past fourteen months and eighteen days, the City has been engaged 
in a strategic planning process with GLM Design Group to update the 1988 Dry Creek Drainage 
Master Plan; 
 
WHEREAS, on September 11, 2023, the City will hold a public hearing to review and on 
accept the Plan; and, 
 
WHEREAS, GLM Design Group has provided the City with a complete copy of the Plan 
that provides risk analysis and recommendations for implementable actions to prepare and 
mitigate areas of concern for loss of life and reduction of annualized flood disaster damages 
within the one-hundred-year (100) floodplain of the city limits. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan submitted by 
GLM, Design Group be finalized and accepted as complete. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
(SEAL)  
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
4:42 pm, Aug 22 2023

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., Deputy City Engineer 
 
DATE: 
August 8, 2023 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wilson & 
Company, Inc. for the Pershing Blvd. and Windmill Rd. Intersection and Signal 
Design Project.  
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wilson 
& Company for engineering services related to the Pershing Blvd. and Windmill Rd. Intersection 
and Signal Design Project. The design will provide intersection geometric modifications, ADA 
facility upgrades, and the reconstruction of outdated signals at the location. Construction 
management services for construction oversight on the project are also included in the contract. 
  
Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during design 
operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this agreement will 
provide the required services to complete the design portion of the project. 
 
The fees include services for project management, intersection re-design, topographical surveying, 
geotechnical investigations, utility investigation, traffic signal design, plan creation, and 
construction management. Funding for this project will come from the 2019 1% Specific Purpose 
Tax Fund (5th Penny). The total professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed $180,182. We 
respectfully request your approval of this professional services agreement.

Project Name:  Pershing Boulevard and Windmill Road Intersection and Signal Design Project 
 
Page 3 of 14 
 
A.1 Overview 
 
Consultant services for the Pershing Boulevard and Windmill Road Intersection Design Project are the 
development and design of a complete set of construction documents, including plans, specifications, and 
estimates. Any innovative solutions that are cost effective and meet project goals will be incorporated 
during project scoping negotiations and included in the design.  Work required for this project will include 
project management; intersection design; signal design; right-of-way surveying; utility location, 
coordination, and design; and construction management. 
The project requires a professional design team that includes a Civil Engineer registered in the State of 
Wyoming with a minimum of 10 years of project design experience in one or more of the following 
disciplines: intersection design and signal design; with the ability to provide in-house or capacity to sub-
consult for survey services and those disciplines required for the project.   
 
A.2 Project Description 
 
A.2.a Overview 
 
The project is located at the intersection of Pershing Boulevard and Windmill Road. 
Intersection and traffic signal design will be the primary tasks for this project. The intersection and approach 
layout should be determined from traffic projections included in the Connect 2045 Cheyenne Area 
Transportation Master Plan.  
 
A.2.b Project History 
 
Intersection modification and signal upgrades are necessary due to both outdated and aged signal 
components and poor intersection operating capacity with peak hour traffic volumes from students 
leaving East High School. 
 
 A.2.c Existing Facilities 
 
Windmill Road terminates at Pershing Boulevard. Pershing Boulevard extends to the east and west of the 
intersection. An alleyway runs parallel to Pershing Boulevard. Currently the intersection is signalized. 
The signals are reaching the end of their design life and are in need of being upgraded. Residential 
housing is situated at the end of this tee intersection and there have been several instances of vehicles 
leaving the roadway and hitting structures located in the back yards of the adjacent parcels. 
  

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  Pershing Boulevard and Windmill Road Intersection and Signal Design Project 
 
Page 4 of 14 
 
A.2.d Proposed Improvements 
 
The proposed improvements at the location are as follows: 
• 
Traffic signal design. 
• 
Intersection design. 
o Evaluation of an additional left turn lane on southbound Windmill Road to eastbound 
Pershing. 
o Evaluation of relocating a City Transit stop to place it closer to the intersection to 
encourage pedestrians to cross at the controlled intersection. 
• 
ADA upgrades. 
• 
Innovative ways to either deter or inhibit vehicles from leaving the roadway and colliding with 
private property south of the intersection. 
 
A.2.e Right-Of-Way 
 
No additional right-of-way is anticipated for this project. 
 
A.2.f Project Funding and Timing 
 
This project will be paid for entirely from 5th penny tax fund. 
The design phase of the project is anticipated to be completed within ten (10) months from the date of 
execution of a Professional Services Agreement with the selected firm. 
 
A.3 General Conditions 
 
The following items are included as part of the basic compensation for this project: 
• 
Normal business expenses - phone, postage, etc. 
• 
Cost of insurance. 
• 
In-house computer time and service. 
• 
Word processing, accounting, and man-hour records. 
• 
Permits and license fees. 
• 
Mileage. 
• 
Travel fees, room and board, per diem. 
• 
Printing costs for all standard review, bidding, and as-constructed plans and other correspondence 
and contract documents. 
• 
Film and processing.
