# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-08-08_TotalExtraction_16243923.pdf
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
 
Subject: Real Estate Purchase Agreement between Trace G. Lewark and Mary J. Lewark and 
the City of Cheyenne for the purchase of real property located at 700 Gettysburg 
Drive, City of Cheyenne, consisting of approximately 189,921.6 square feet, more 
or less and associated access and utility easements. 
 
Date: 
August 23, 2023 
 
Background:  
The purpose of this Real Estate Purchase Agreement is to purchase property for the Sun Valley 
to Kiwanis Park Greenway connection.  The land will also be used as Land and Water 
Conservation Fund (LWCF) conversion property for the 2.5-acre site of the new fire station 
being constructed on Converse Avenue.  The appraised value of the property is $245,000.00 
and funding will come from 2021 6th Penny for Greenway Expansion.  For the remaining Lewark 
property a 40-foot width shared access easement will be granted to the City and for the City 
owned property a 10-foot width utility easement will be granted to the Lewarks. 
 
Enclosures: 
Real Estate Purchase Agreement 
Easement Agreements and Exhibits 
Sun Valley to East (Kiwanis) Park Corridor Exhibit 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

REAL ESTATE PURCHASE AGREEMENT  
 
 
THIS REAL ESTATE PURCHASE AGREEMENT (the “Agreement”) is made and 
entered into this ________ day of ____________________, 2023, by and between  Trace G. 
Lewark & Mary J. Lewark, 814 McGovern Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (the “Seller”), 
and the City of Cheyenne, a municipality organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (the “City”). 
 
WITNESSETH: 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City wishes to secure, for public purposes, the real property legally 
described as: 
 
A tract of Land situated in the SE¼SE¼ and the SW¼SE¼ of Section 35, 
Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
more particularly described as follows: 
 
Commencing at the southwest corner of said Section 35; thence 77°50'00", grid 
azimuth from North, City of Cheyenne Datum, a distance of 2406.81 feet to the 
point of beginning, said point also being a point on the South line of Sun Valley 
Addition Fourteenth Filing; thence 55°40'30", along the southerly line of said Sun 
Valley Addition Fourteenth Filing, a distance of 457.14 feet; thence 145°32'22", a 
distance of 129.07 feet; 51°52'57", a distance of 567.85 feet; thence 325°40'30", a 
distance of 91.51 feet, to a point on the South line of said Sun Valley Addition 
Fourteenth Filing; thence 55°40'30", along said South line, a distance of 558.51 
feet, to the West line of Chukker Ridge 2nd Filing; thence 145°33'02", along said 
West line, a distance of 160.26 feet; thence 235°43'23", a distance of 1582.13 
feet; thence 325°23'33"; a distance of 158.94 feet to the point of beginning. 
Containing 4.36 acres more or less. 
 
together with the Seller’s rights, privileges, interests and appurtenances related to 
said real property, including, but not limited to, all right, title and interest in and 
to adjacent streets, alleys, and rights-of-way, all of which is hereinafter referred to 
as the “Real Property;” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Seller is the owner of the above-described Real Property which is 
generally located at 700 Gettysburg Drive, City of Cheyenne, consisting of approximately 
189,921.6 square feet, more or less; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Seller is willing to sell to the City and the City is willing to purchase 
from Seller the Real Property upon the terms and conditions hereinafter stated. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration, the receipt of which is hereby 
acknowledged, the parties hereto agree as follows: 
 
5:05 pm, Aug 03 2023

 
Page 2 of  12 
1. 
Purchase and Sale. Seller agrees to sell to the City and the City agrees to purchase from 
Seller: 
 
 
1.1. 
The Real Property, described above. 
 
 
1.2. 
All licenses, permits and franchises issued by any governmental authority to or in 
favor of Seller and pertaining to the ownership, development or use of the Real Property, if any; 
 
 
1.3. 
All contract rights and general intangible rights pertaining to the ownership, 
development or use of the Real Property, if any; 
 
 
1.4. 
All improvements, structures and fixtures situated on the Real Property which will 
be conveyed in an “as is” condition without any representations or warranties relating to their 
physical condition and without ascribing any value to them; 
 
 
1.5. 
All fixtures, equipment and other personal property of every kind and nature now 
installed, located, situated or used in, on, or about, or in connection with the operation, use, or 
enjoyment of the Real Property; and, 
 
 
1.6. 
All:  (I) contracts or agreements affecting or relating to the Real Property, but 
only to the extent the City elects to take an assignment thereof; (ii) licenses, permits or similar 
documents; (iii) plans, drawings, specifications, surveys, engineering reports, and other technical 
reports or descriptions of the Real Property, to the extent Seller has any in its possession; and (iv) 
insurance contracts or policies which pertain to the Real Property, but only to the extent the City 
elects to take an assignment thereof. 
 
 
1.7. 
Prior to closing, the Seller shall pay, when due and payable, all real property taxes 
and assessments asserted against the Real Property for the current year and for all prior years.  
All such taxes and assessments for the current year will be apportioned between the parties on a 
pro rata basis as of the date of closing.   
 
2. 
Purchase Price. 
 
 
2.1. 
The purchase price to be paid by the City to Seller for the Real Property and all of 
the Seller’s interests in said Real Property, as described in Section 1 of this Agreement, is two 
hundred forty-five thousand dollars ($245,000) (the “Purchase Price”), subject to proration and 
adjustments as provided herein. 
 
 
2.2. 
The Purchase Price shall be paid to Seller or, with written authorization, to 
Seller’s designee, in full at the time of Closing, subject to pro-rations and adjustments, provided, 
however, that the City’s duty to pay the Purchase Price at Closing is subject to the requirement 
that the Seller complete an IRS Form W-9 and otherwise cooperates with the City in reporting 
the Purchase Price and the other terms and conditions of this Agreement to the Internal Revenue 
Service as required by law. 
 

 
Page 3 of 12 
 
2.3. 
Prior to the date of Closing, Seller shall secure the release and discharge of all 
mortgages, liens, assessments, leases, rental agreements, advertising sign leases, if any, and other 
claims which encumber the Real Property.  The City shall have the right to cancel this 
Agreement in the event Seller is unable or otherwise fails, neglects or refuses to convey good 
and marketable title to the City in accordance with the terms and conditions of this Agreement. 
 
 
2.4 
After the date of Closing, the Parties agree that, upon construction of the 
Greenway adjacent to the Seller’s remaining real property, the City agrees to purchase and 
construct a six (6) foot privacy fence along the length of Seller’s adjoining property that abuts 
the Greenway. 
 
3. 
Title. 
 
 
3.1. 
Prior to Closing, the City shall have the right to obtain a Title Commitment for the 
Real Property (the “Title Commitment”) at the City’s sole cost and expense.  In the event the 
Title Commitment shows that the Seller is not vested with good, marketable and insurable fee 
simple title to the Real Property, or that the Real Property is subject to liens, encumbrances, 
taxes or assessments which will not be discharged prior to Closing, the City shall have the right, 
in its sole and absolute discretion, to declare this Agreement null and void pursuant to Section 
3.2 of this Agreement.  Alternatively, the City may accept the Real Property, subject to such 
matters as set forth on Exhibit “A” attached to this Agreement (collectively, the “Permitted 
Exceptions”).  The condition of title as described in this Section 3.1 shall be the standard of 
marketable title that Seller shall deliver at the Closing.  If Seller delivers such title and except for 
the Permitted Exceptions as set forth in Exhibit “A”, such title will be satisfactory and acceptable 
to the City under Sections 3.4 and 7.2 herein. 
 
 
3.2. 
Prior to Closing, the City shall have the right to obtain a survey (the “Survey”) of 
the Real Property, which Survey shall be prepared at the City’s sole cost and expense.  Seller 
shall grant to the City, and its employees and agents, access to the Real Property sufficient for 
the preparation of the Survey, including without limitation:  (I) the actual dimensions of, and 
area within, the Real Property, (ii) the location of any easements, setback lines, encroachments, 
or overlaps thereon and the outside boundary lines of all improvements; (iii) all easements, 
setback lines, and other matters referred to on the Title Commitment; (iv) access to and from the 
Real Property from any publicly dedicated streets or roads; and (v) any areas within the Real 
Property that have been designated by any federal, state or other governmental agency or body as 
being subject to special, or increased flooding hazards.  If required by the City, the Survey shall 
be prepared and certified by a surveyor licensed in the State of Wyoming and, if required, shall 
be sufficient to allow issuance of an ALTA Extended Coverage Title Policy. 
 
 
3.3. 
Within five (5) business days following the execution of this Agreement, Seller 
shall deliver to the City the following: 
 
 
(a) 
True, complete and legible copies of all documents referred to in the Title 
Commitment to the extent that any such document has not been filed for record in the office of 
the Laramie County Clerk and ex-officio register of deeds; 

 
Page 4 of  12 
 
 
(b) 
True, complete and legible copies of all documents that are known to Seller, that 
are not referred to in the Title Commitment, and that may affect the marketability of title to the 
Real Property; 
  
 
(c) 
Any soils, geological, and engineering studies; 
 
 
(d) 
Copies of any entitlements or permits relating to the development of the Real 
Property;  
 
 
(e) 
True and complete copies or originals of any lease or occupancy agreements for 
any portion of the Real Property; 
 
 
(f) 
Copies of environmental studies in the possession of Seller pertaining to the Real 
Property;  
 
 
(g) 
Any notices of violations of law received by Seller during the period of time that 
Seller has owned the Real Property; 
 
 
(h) 
All mortgages, notes and loan documents for any mortgage which encumbers the 
Real Property on the date hereof, which mortgages, notes, and loan documents shall be 
discharged by the Seller prior to closing; and 
 
 
(i) 
Copies of any and all materials relating to pending or present litigation involving 
the Real Property.  
 
 
3.4. 
The City shall have twenty (20) business days from the date of receipt of the Title 
Commitment referred to in Section 3.1 or twenty (20) business days following receipt of 
documents to be provided by the Seller pursuant to Section 3.3, whichever period is longer, in 
which to examine the same.  If the City finds title to the Real Property to be other than as 
described in Section 3.1, hereof, the City shall, no later than the expiration of said period of 
twenty (20) business days, notify Seller in writing specifying the defect or defects; provided that, 
if the City fails to give Seller written  notice of such defects before the expiration of said period 
of twenty (20) business days, the defects shown in the Title Commitment or in documents 
provided by Seller pursuant to Section 3.3 shall be deemed to be Permitted Exceptions and 
waived as title objections. The City may raise as additional objections, however, any defect first 
shown by any title update between the date of the Title Commitment and the Closing Date by 
giving Seller written notice of any such defect within five (5) business days after the City first 
has notice of same. If the City has given Seller timely written notice of defects that render the 
title other than as required by Section 3.1 of this Agreement (”Defects”), and only in the event 
that the Defects were the result of Seller’s action or caused by Seller’s failure to act, Seller shall 
use its best efforts to cause such Defects to be cured within ten (10) business days following 
receipt of such notice.  In the event the Seller fails to eliminate such Defects, the City shall have 
the right to terminate this Agreement pursuant to Section 5.1. 
 

 
Page 5 of 12 
 
3.5. 
In the event the Seller creates any liens or encumbrances on the Real Property 
which are capable of being satisfied by the payment of money at the Closing, Seller shall be 
obligated to use the Purchase Price to cure the Defects. 
 
 
3.6. 
The date for elimination of the Defects may be extended until Closing.  In the 
event that Seller does not eliminate any Defect prior to or at the Closing, as the same may be 
extended under the preceding provision, the City shall have the option of either: (I) closing and 
accepting the title “as is,” without reduction in the Purchase Price and without claim against 
Seller therefor, or (ii) cancelling this Agreement.  In the event of a Defect that was not caused by 
Seller’s action or caused by Seller’s failure to act, Seller’s only responsibility shall be to assign 
to the City Seller’s Title Insurance Policy or Seller’s rights under Seller’s Title Insurance Policy, 
whichever is applicable, and cooperate with the City in obtaining the assistance of Seller’s title 
insurer to remove the Defect. 
 
 
3.7. 
Seller shall not create any new exceptions to title, and shall use its best efforts to 
prevent any other person or entity from creating new exceptions to title to the Real Property prior 
to Closing. 
 
4. 
Inspection Period. 
 
 
4.1. 
The City shall have thirty (30) calendar days following the date of execution of 
this Agreement (the “Inspection Period”) to make such physical, zoning, marketing and other 
investigations, tests and studies, including but not limited to soil tests and borings, Phase I 
environmental surveys, utility availability surveys, and confirmation of subdivision map 
requirements and restrictions as the City may deem appropriate (“the City’s Inspections”).  The 
City’s Inspections shall be at the City’s sole cost and expense.  The City shall repair any damage 
to the Real Property caused by the City’s Inspections, reasonable wear and tear excepted.  The 
City shall indemnify Seller and hold Seller harmless from any loss, cost or expense arising 
during or as a result of the City’s Inspections. 
 
 
4.2. 
The City, at its option, may conduct, at its sole cost, such independent 
investigation and inspection of the Property as the City shall deem reasonably necessary to 
ascertain the “environmental” condition of the Property (“the City’s Environmental 
Investigation”).  The City assumes no responsibility or liability as a result of any inaccuracies 
arising in or from the City’s Environmental Investigation, and makes no representations or 
warranties whatsoever regarding (I) the completeness of the City’s Environmental Investigation, 
or (ii) the truth or accuracy of the City’s Environmental Investigation.  If Seller conducts any 
environmental testing of the Real Property subsequent to the date of this Agreement, Seller shall 
provide the City with a copy of any report promptly after its receipt of same. Seller shall have no 
obligation to remedy any environmental problem on the Real Property. 
 
5. 
Termination. 
 
 
5.1. 
In the event the City, in the City’s sole and absolute discretion, determines that 
any matter shown in the City’s Inspections or Environmental Investigation is not satisfactory 

 
Page 6 of  12 
either during or subsequent to the Inspection Period, the City shall have the right, at any time 
prior to the Closing, to terminate this Agreement by giving Seller written notice thereof (the 
“Termination Notice”), in which event, both parties shall be released from all further obligations 
under this Agreement. 
 
6. 
Conditions Precedent. 
 
 
6.1. 
The City’s obligation to close this transaction shall be subject to fulfillment of the 
following conditions precedent: 
 
 
(a) 
Seller shall, at Seller’s sole cost and expense, obtain releases, at or prior to 
Closing, for any and all liens or encumbrances affecting the Real Property caused by Seller’s 
action or caused by Seller’s failure to act as of the date of Closing, pursuant to the terms of this 
Agreement; 
 
 
(b) 
Seller shall not be in default in its obligations under this Agreement; and 
 
 
(c) 
The City, at City’s cost, obtains a title insurance commitment and Title Insurance 
Policy, in a form acceptable to the City (the “Title Policy”), insuring the City’s title to the Real 
Property in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
 
6.2. 
In the event any of the foregoing conditions precedent are not fulfilled as of 
Closing (or earlier date where specified otherwise), then the City shall have the option of either:  
(I) waiving the condition and closing “as is” without reduction in the Purchase Price or claim 
against Seller therefor, provided that Seller assigns to the City all of Seller’s right, title and 
interest to the title insurance policy it obtained when it acquired the Property, or (ii) cancelling 
this Agreement by written notice to Seller. 
 
7. 
Seller’s Representations and Warranties. 
 
 
7.1. 
Seller hereby represents and warrants to the City that the following are true 
statements as of the date of this Agreement and shall be true as of the date of Closing, and Seller 
agrees to deliver a certificate confirming that the following representations and warranties are 
true as of the date of the Closing. 
 
 
(a) 
Seller is an individual or is a legal entity organized, existing and in good standing 
under the laws of the State of Wyoming, and is authorized to do business in the State of 
Wyoming, and Seller has full power and authority to sell and convey the Real Property and to 
execute this Agreement and such other documents described herein to be executed by Seller. 
 
 
(b) 
Seller now has and will have at Closing good, marketable and indefeasible title in 
fee simple to the Real Property subject to the Permitted Exceptions and no party, except as 
herein set forth, has or shall have any right in, or to acquire, the Property. 
 
 
(c) 
At Closing, the Real Property shall be free and clear of all encumbrances except 
the Permitted Exceptions as set forth in Article 3 of this Agreement. 

 
Page 7 of 12 
 
 
(d) 
At Closing, the Real Property shall be free from any and all actions, suits, claims, 
assessments or proceedings pending or, to the knowledge of Seller, threatened that could 
materially adversely affect the ownership, operation, or maintenance of the Real Property or 
Seller’s ability to perform hereunder.  
 
 
(e) 
To the best of Seller’s knowledge, the Property and Seller are not currently in 
violation of or subject to:  (I) any existing, pending or threatened investigation or inquiry by any 
governmental authority; or (ii) any remedial obligations under any Applicable Laws pertaining to 
health or the environment (hereinafter sometimes collectively called “Applicable Environmental 
Laws”), 
including 
without 
limitation 
the 
Comprehensive 
Environmental 
Response, 
Compensation, and Liability Act of 1980, as amended (”CERCLA”), and the Resource 
Conservation and Recovery Act of 1976, as amended (”RCRA”).  Seller shall have no obligation 
to remove or take any action to cure or remediate any environmentally hazardous material from 
the Real Property if any environmentally hazardous material is found on the Real Property. 
 
 
(f) 
Seller does not have knowledge of any hazardous substances or solid wastes that 
have been disposed of or otherwise released on the Real Property.  The terms “hazardous 
substance” and “release” shall have the meanings specified in CERCLA, and the terms “solid 
waste” and “disposal” or “disposed” shall have the meanings specified in RCRA; provided, to 
the extent that the laws of the State of Wyoming establish a meaning for “hazardous substance,” 
“release,” “solid waste,” or “disposal” that is broader than that specified in either CERCLA or 
RCRA, such broader meaning shall apply. 
 
 
(g) 
There are no parties in possession of any portion of the Real Property, whether as 
lessees, tenants at sufferance, trespassers or otherwise. 
 
 
7.2. 
Seller shall not intentionally cause any of the representations and warranties 
contained herein to become materially incorrect, nor authorize any action which would do so. If 
subsequent to the execution of this Agreement and on or before Closing, Seller nonetheless has 
knowledge that any of the representations and warranties set forth herein have become incorrect 
in any respect (a “Discrepancy”), Seller shall promptly notify the City, in writing, of the 
occurrence of such event.  The City shall have a right to approve or disapprove any such 
Discrepancy, which approval or disapproval shall be given by written notice delivered to Seller 
within ten (10) business days prior to the Closing for the City to evaluate the effect of such 
Discrepancy.  If the City waives such Discrepancy, and elects to acquire the Real Property, 
Seller’s representations and warranties contained herein shall be deemed to have been made as of 
the Closing, subject to the waived Discrepancy.  If the City disapproves of the Discrepancy, and 
Seller fails to cure same, then upon written notice thereof to Seller this Agreement shall be 
terminated and all rights and obligations of the parties hereunder shall terminate. 
  
 
7.3. 
Except for the representations of Seller set forth in Section 7.1, that shall survive 
for one (1) year from the Closing, the express representations and warranties made in this 
Agreement shall merge into any instrument or conveyance delivered at Closing and shall not 
survive Closing. 

 
Page 8 of  12 
8. 
Risk of Loss. 
 
 
8.1. 
The Property shall be conveyed to the City in substantially and materially the 
same condition as on the date this Agreement is executed by the City, ordinary wear and tear 
excepted, free of all tenancies or occupancies. 
 
9. 
Default Provisions. 
 
 
9.1. 
In the event of the wrongful failure or refusal of the City to close this transaction 
after sixty (60) calendar day’s written notice, Seller shall be entitled to those remedies as may be 
provided by law. 
 
 
9.2. 
In the event of Seller’s failure to close in accordance with the terms and 
provisions of this Agreement for sixty (60) calendar days after written notice, the City, at its 
option and as the City’s sole and exclusive remedy for default of Seller, shall have the right 
either to terminate this Agreement or seek specific performance of the Seller’s obligations 
hereunder. 
 
10. 
Prorations.  All pro-ratable items, if any, shall be prorated as of the date of Closing. 
 
11. 
Closing Costs and Expenses.  At Closing, the City shall pay the premium for the Title 
Insurance Policy to be issued to the City.  The City shall pay all costs associated with surveys, 
inspections and environmental assessments conducted by the City.  The City shall pay recording 
fees necessary to record instruments received by the City at closing and one-half of 
miscellaneous closing costs and fees charged by the title insurance company selected by the 
parties to close this transaction.  Each party shall pay that party’s attorney’s fees, if any.  Seller 
shall pay recording costs with respect to documents necessary to clear title at Closing and all 
instruments received by Seller at closing. 
 
12. 
Closing. 
 
 
12.1. The closing of this transaction shall be held on the first business day occurring 
thirty (30) calendar days after the satisfaction of all of the conditions of Closing (the “Closing 
Date”).  The title insurance company selected by the parties shall prepare the closing documents. 
 
 
12.2. At Closing, Seller shall deliver: only to the Permitted Exceptions;  
 
(a) 
A warranty deed duly executed and acknowledged in proper form for recording 
conveying to the City good and indefeasible fee simple title in and to the Real Property;  
 
 
(b) 
An assignment of all rights appurtenant to the Real Property, if any, as provided 
in Section 1, above; 
 
 
(c) 
A title affidavit and a mechanic’s lien affidavit; 
 
 
(d) 
An affidavit of exclusive possession; 

 
Page 9 of 12 
 
 
(e) 
An affidavit pursuant to Internal Revenue Code Section 1445(b)(2) with respect 
to Seller’s status as a non-foreign person; 
 
 
(f) 
A certificate, or other document acceptable to the City, confirming Seller’s 
authority to sell the Real Property; and 
 
 
(g) 
Such other documents as are usually required to insure the City’s title. 
 
 
12.3. At closing, the City, at the City’s expense, shall receive an Owner’s Title 
Insurance Policy (”Owner’s Title Policy”), in a form acceptable to the City, issued by a title 
insurance company authorized to do business in the State of Wyoming, in the full amount of the 
Purchase Price, insuring the City’s indefeasible fee simple title to the Real Property, subject only 
to the Permitted Exceptions and the printed exceptions contained in the standard form of the 
Owner’s Title Insurance Policy. 
 
13. 
Brokers.  The parties each represent and warrant to the other that no party to this 
transaction shall be responsible for the payment of any compensation or commission due any real 
estate agent, broker, salesman or finder unless that party has engaged the real estate agent, 
broker, salesman, or finder in writing.  If a claim for compensation or commission in connection 
with this transaction is made by any agent, broker, salesman or finder claiming to have dealt 
through or on behalf of one of the parties hereto, such party shall indemnify, defend and hold 
harmless the other party  hereunder from and against all liabilities, damages, claims, costs, fees 
and expenses whatsoever (including reasonable attorney’s fees and court costs at all trial and 
appellate levels) with respect to said claim for compensation or commission. Anything to the 
contrary notwithstanding, the provisions of this paragraph shall survive the Closing and any 
cancellation or termination of this Agreement. 
 
14. 
Assignment.  Neither party shall have the right to assign this Agreement without the 
express written consent of the other Party. 
 
15. 
Access.  The City, and the City’s agents and contractors, shall have the right during the 
term of this Agreement to enter upon the Real Property at any time for purposes of making 
investigations, tests and studies, provided that the City shall indemnify, defend, and hold 
harmless Seller from and against all liabilities, damages, claims, costs, fees and expenses 
whatsoever (including reasonable attorney’s fees and court costs at all trial and appellate levels) 
for bodily injury, death, or physical damage to the Real Property caused by any such 
investigation test or study. The City shall provide written notification to the Seller at least three 
(3) business days prior to entering upon the Real Property for the purposes of making 
investigations, tests or studies. The foregoing indemnification shall survive the Closing and any 
cancellation or termination of this Agreement. 
 
16. 
Notices.  Any notices required or permitted to be given under this Agreement shall be 
delivered by hand or mailed by certified or registered mail, return receipt requested, or overnight 
delivery service in postage prepaid envelope, and addressed as follows:

If to the City:   
City of Cheyenne 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
ATTN:  City Parks and Greenway Planner 
 
 
 
With copy to:  City Attorney 
  
 
If to the Seller:  
Trace G. Lewark & Mary J Lewark  
 
 
 
 
814 McGovern Avenue 
 
 
 
 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
17. 
Operation of the Property. 
 
 
17.1. During the period between the date hereof and the Closing, Seller shall: 
 
 
(a) 
Keep the Real Property in substantially the same repair and condition as of the 
date hereof, but excluding damages to the Property caused by the City’s inspections or surveys; 
 
 
(b) 
Comply with all state and municipal laws, ordinances, regulations, and orders 
relating to the Real Property; 
 
 
(c) 
Comply with all the terms, conditions, and provisions of all liens, mortgages, 
agreements, insurance policies, and other contractual arrangements relating to the Real Property, 
make all payments due thereunder, and suffer no default therein; 
 
 
(d) 
Without written approval of the City, neither negotiate nor enter into any new 
contract or modify any existing contract affecting the use or operation of the Real Property 
which cannot be terminated without charge, cost, penalty, or premium on or before the Closing; 
 
 
(e) 
Operate, manage, and maintain the Real Property in the usual and customary 
manner for similar property; and 
 
 
(f) 
Promptly notify the City in writing if any material change occurs in the 
occupancy or conditions affecting the Real Property. 
 
18. 
Miscellaneous. 
 
 
18.1. This Agreement shall be construed and governed in accordance with the laws of 
the State of Wyoming.  Except for enforcement of the terms and conditions of this Agreement as 
between the parties, the City reserves all defenses and immunities under the Wyoming 
Governmental Claims Act, W.S. § 1-39-101, et seq., and other applicable constitutional and 
statutory provisions of the State of Wyoming.  All legal proceedings shall occur in a court of 
competent jurisdiction sitting in Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 

 
Page 11 of 12 
 
18.2. In the event any term or provision of this Agreement be determined by 
appropriate judicial authority to be illegal or otherwise invalid, such provision shall be given its 
nearest legal meaning or be construed as deleted as such authority determines, and the remainder 
of this Agreement shall be construed to be in full force and effect. 
 
 
18.3. In construing this Agreement, the singular shall be held to include the plural, the 
plural shall be held to include the singular, the use of any gender shall be held to include every 
other and all genders, and captions and paragraph headings shall be disregarded. 
 
 
18.4. All of the exhibits attached to this Agreement are incorporated in, and made a part 
of, this Agreement. 
 
 
18.5. This Agreement constitutes the entire understanding and agreement between the 
parties, and there are no understandings, agreements, representations or warranties except as 
specifically set forth herein. 
 
 
18.6. This Agreement may not be changed, altered or modified except by an instrument 
in writing signed by the party against whom enforcement of such change would be sought. 
 
 
18.7. This Agreement shall be binding upon the parties hereto and their respective 
successors and assigns. 
 
 
18.8. The failure of any party at any time or times to require performance of any 
provision hereof shall in no manner affect its right at a later time to enforce the same.  No waiver 
by any party of any condition, or of any breach of any covenant, agreement, representation or 
warranty contained in this Agreement, in any one or more instances, shall be deemed to be or 
construed as a further or continuing waiver of any such condition or breach or waiver of any 
other condition or of any breach of any other covenant, agreement, representation or warranty. 
 
 
18.9. If the final date of any period provided for herein for the performance of an 
obligation or for the taking of any action falls on a Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday, then the 
time of that period shall be deemed extended to the next day which is not a Saturday, Sunday or 
legal holiday. All time periods expiring on a specific date or period herein shall be deemed to 
expire at 5:00 p.m. Mountain Standard or Mountain Daylight Time, as applicable. 
 
 
18.10. This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of which, 
when so executed and delivered, shall be deemed original, but such counterparts together shall 
constitute but one agreement. 
 
 
18.11. During the term of this Agreement, Seller shall not list the Real Property for sale, 
rent or exchange or otherwise offer the Real Property for sale, rent or exchange.  Seller shall 
have the right to disclose this Agreement to any lending institution.

UTILITY EASEMENT AGREEMENT 
THIS UTILITY EASEMENT AGREEMENT ("Agreement") is made as of the ______ day of 
___________________, 2023 ("Effective Date"), by and between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a duly-
organized municipal corporation, having an address at 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
("Grantor"), and Trace G. and Mary J. Lewark, having an address at 814 McGovern Avenue, Cheyenne, 
WY  82001 ("Grantee", and collectively referred to with Grantor as, the "Parties"). 
RECITALS 
A. Grantor is the fee owner of that certain real property located at 700 Gettysburg Drive, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and more particularly described at Exhibit A attached hereto (“10-foot utility easement 
area"). 
B. 
Grantor desires to convey to Grantee, its successors and assigns, the right to use and occupy the 
Easement Area for future utilities and other ancillary purposes. 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the sum of Ten Dollars ($10.00) other good and valuable 
consideration paid by Grantee to Grantor, and the mutual covenants, terms, and conditions set forth herein, 
the receipt and sufficiency of which is hereby acknowledged, the Parties agree as follows: 
1. Incorporation of Recitals. The foregoing recitals are true and correct and are expressly incorporated 
herein by this reference. 
2. Grant of Easement. Grantor hereby grants and conveys to Grantee, its legal representatives, 
successors and assigns, and to any future owner of the Benefitted Property, an irrevocable, 
non-exclusive, appurtenant utility easement (the "Easement") in, under, upon, about, over, and 
through the Easement Area for the benefit of the Benefitted Property, for purposes of 
installation, use, maintenance and repairs to a future private water service within the easement 
area; and any and all other ancillary purposes.  
3. Easement Description.  
A 10-foot utility easement situated in the S1/2 of Section 35, Township 14 North, Range 66 
West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, extending 5 feet to each side of the 
following described centerline: 
Beginning at a point from which the Southwest corner of said Section 35 bears 254°26'26", 
a distance of 2837.03 feet, thence 145°40'30", parallel and 5 feet distant from the South line 
of Sun Valley Addition 14th Filing, a distance of 302.30 feet and said easement there 
terminates. 
4. Duration. This Easement shall remain in full force and effect in perpetuity. 
5. Use of Easement Area. The Easement, rights, and privileges set forth herein shall be for the benefit 
of Grantee and binding on its successors and assigns, and each of its respective tenants, licensees, 
customers, employees, agents, invitees, successors and assigns. The Easement, rights, privileges, 
restrictions, benefits and obligations hereunder shall be covenants running with the land. The 
covenants set forth in this Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of Grantor, its 
successors, and assigns.

Page 2 of 5 
 
6. Access. Grantee, its employees, contractors, and agents shall have the right of access to the 
Easement Area twenty-four (24) hours per day, seven (7) days per week. 
7.  Repairs and Maintenance. During the Term, Grantee shall maintain the Easement Area in good 
order and condition, and in compliance with applicable governmental laws, ordinances, rules, and 
regulations. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Grantor shall continue to maintain, throughout the 
Term, all such portions of the Easement Area that would typically be maintained by Grantor in 
connection with its obligations for maintenance of public rights-of-way. 
8. Damage and Destruction. 
a. Neither Grantor nor Grantee shall have any responsibility to the other or their respective 
agents, contractors, tenants, or other invitees in the event of any damage to or theft or loss 
of any equipment or property of the other party and the party incurring such damage, theft, 
or loss shall look to its own insurance coverage (and to any self-insured portion of the 
damage, theft, or loss), if any, for recovery in the event of any such damage, theft, or loss. 
b. If all, or a portion, of the Easement Area is destroyed or damaged by fire or other casualty, 
Grantor shall promptly proceed to repair such damage and restore the Easement Area to 
the condition existing prior to such damage. 
9. Indemnification. Each Party to this Agreement assumes the risk of any liability arising from its own 
conduct.  Each Party agrees that it will be responsible for any loss, claim, damages, or demands 
resulting from its negligence and the negligence of its employees and agents.  Neither Party agrees 
to insure, defend, or indemnify the other. 
10. Governmental/Sovereign Immunity. Grantee does not waive its Governmental/Sovereign 
Immunity, as provided by any applicable law including Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., by 
entering into this Agreement. Further, Grantor fully retains all immunities and defenses 
provided by law with regard to any action, whether in tort, contract or any other theory of law 
based on this Agreement, with the express exception for an action brought to enforce the terms 
of this Agreement. 
11. Miscellaneous. 
a. Entire Agreement. This Agreement represents the entire and integrated agreement and 
understanding between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, statements, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
b. Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in two or more counterparts, each of which 
shall be deemed to be an original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the 
same instrument. 
c. Governing Law. The Parties mutually understand and agree this Agreement shall be 
governed by and interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming. 
d. Section Headings. The section titles herein are for convenience only and do not define, 
limit, or construe the contents of such sections. 
e. Attachment and Exhibits. All attachments and exhibits to this Agreement are hereby made 
a part hereof as if fully set out herein.

f. Severability. If any provision or provisions in this Agreement is/are found to be in violation 
of any law or otherwise unenforceable, all other provisions remain unaffected in full force

g. Binding Effect. This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the parties 
hereto and their respective successors and permitted assigns and shall not be amended,

CONTRACT NO. ___________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
 
 
This Agreement is entered into this _______ day of ____________________, 2023, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of 
Wyoming, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter 
referred to as the “City,” and  Volunteers of American Northern Rockies, Alcohol Receiving 
Center, whose address is 1876 S. Sheridan Ave, Sheridan, WY 82801, hereinafter referred to as 
the “Contractor.” 
 
W I T N E S S E T H: 
 
WHEREAS, the City has need for certain human services in the community; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Contractor is capable and desirous of performing said services. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties mutually agree as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF SERVICES:  The Contractor shall, in a satisfactory and proper manner 
as determined by the City, perform the obligations set forth in Appendix A, attached hereto and 
incorporated by this reference, hereinafter referred to as the “Program.” 
 
2. 
TERM:  The Contractor shall commence performance of this Agreement on the 1st 
day of July 2023 and shall complete performance to the satisfaction of the City no later than the 
30th day of June 2024. 
 
3. 
FEES:  The City agrees to pay Contractor a total amount not to exceed One 
Hundred Thirty-Six Thousand, Eight Hundred Seventy Five ($136,875) for the services set 
forth in Appendix A upon acceptance of performance by the City.  Payment is to be made in one 
lump sum on or before the 15th day of December 2023.   
 
Payment by the City to the Contractor for the services performed under the terms and 
4:09 pm, Aug 21 2023

 
Page 2 of 10 
conditions of this Agreement is contingent upon the availability of the City funds which are 
appropriated and allocated for payment of such obligation.  The City shall notify the Contractor at 
the earliest possible time of any such shortage or depletion of funds. 
 
4. 
RIGHT TO TERMINATE AGREEMENT:  It is mutually agreed and understood 
that the City reserves the right to terminate this Agreement at any time, provided, however, that 
the City shall pay the Contractor on a pro rata basis for services rendered up to the time of 
cancellation.  In the event of termination, all property and documents, data, studies and reports 
purchased, prepared or related to this Agreement whether completed or in progress shall 
immediately become the property of the City and shall be delivered to the City within five (5) days 
of termination.  Notwithstanding the above, the Contractor shall not be relieved of liability to the 
City for damages sustained by the City because of any breach of this Agreement by the Contractor, 
and the City may withhold any reimbursement to the Contractor for the purpose of set-off until 
such time as the exact amount of damages due the City from the Contractor is agreed upon or 
otherwise determined. In the event the Contractor fails to strictly perform in accordance with this 
Agreement, the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Contractor. 
 
5. 
PUBLICATION AND PUBLICITY:  The Contractor may not publish results, data 
or findings arising from its performance of this Agreement in the absence of the prior written 
approval of the Human Services Advisory Council.  A copy of the publication will be furnished to 
the City. 
 
6. 
WYOMING LAWS GOVERN:  The construction, interpretation and enforcement 
of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming; and the courts of the 
First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising 
out of this Agreement. 

 
Page 3 of 10 
 
7. 
COVENANT AGAINST CONTINGENT FEES:  The Contractor warrants that no 
person, selling agency or other organization has been employed or retained to solicit or secure this 
Agreement upon agreement or understanding for a commission, percentage, brokerage or

to the Cheyenne City Council.

vehicles with minimum limits for bodily injury and property damage of $1,000,000.00 per 
accident for the entire term of this Agreement. 
 
c. 
Workers’ Compensation.  Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in

professional liability insurance or errors and omissions liability insurance in an amount not

VOLUNTEERS OF AMERICAN NORTHERN ROCKIES 
ALCOHOL RECEIVING CENTER 
 
 
Mission: 
A private, not for profit system of care that provides accessible, affordable and 
effective mental health and substance abuse services to the residents of Albany, 
Goshen, Laramie and Platte counties in Wyoming. 
 
 
Program Purpose: 
To provide social, non-medical alcohol and drug detoxification services for 
the well-being and safety of those in need and for the community in general.

THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this ______ day of _________________,

East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001.  
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1.

project, in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated 
by reference and expressly made a part hereof.

specified in Exhibit A, the remaining portion of allocated funds, if any, shall be returned 
to the City and not become part of Consultant operating funds. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE: 
The Scope of Work shall be complete by June 30, 2026. Any extensions of the time limit 
set forth in the scope of work must be agreed upon in writing by the parties. 
 
3.

The Consultant shall provide funding and technical assistance to community programs 
that provide necessary relief to the poor, as provided in the Scope of Work attached 
hereto as Exhibit A. The Consultant shall perform all work contemplated under this 
Agreement in a professional manner in conformance with industry standards.

2024, and an additional $250,000 on or about the first day of October, 2025, and the City 
agrees to pay Consultant according to the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A.  
The total amount of payments shall not exceed $750,000 over the three-year period.  That 
payment amount is based upon City of Cheyenne Resolution No. 6248, “A Resolution

2023 through 2026 and pledging that all revenues derived from this tax will be used for

CONTRACT NO. ___________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
 
 
This Agreement is entered into this _______ day of ____________________, 2023, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of 
Wyoming, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter 
referred to as the “City,” and Safe Harbor, whose address is 2712 Thomes, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001, hereinafter referred to as the “Contractor.” 
 
W I T N E S S E T H: 
 
WHEREAS, the City has need for certain human services in the community; and, 
 
WHEREAS, the Contractor is capable and desirous of performing said services. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties mutually agree as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF SERVICES:  The Contractor shall, in a satisfactory and proper manner 
as determined by the City, perform the obligations set forth in Appendix A, attached hereto and 
incorporated by this reference, hereinafter referred to as the “Program.” 
 
2. 
TERM:  The Contractor shall commence performance of this Agreement on the 1st 
day of July 2023. and shall complete performance to the satisfaction of the City no later than the 
30th day of June 2024. 
 
3. 
FEES:  The City agrees to pay Contractor a total amount not to exceed Twenty 
Thousand Dollars ($20,000) for the services set forth in Appendix A upon acceptance of 
performance by the City.  Payment is to be made in one lump sum on or before the 15th day of 
December 2023.   
 
Payment by the City to the Contractor for the services performed under the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement is contingent upon the availability of the City funds which are 
4:01 pm, Aug 21 2023

 
 
Page 2 of 10 
appropriated and allocated for payment of such obligation.  The City shall notify the Contractor at 
the earliest possible time of any such shortage or depletion of funds. 
 
4. 
RIGHT TO TERMINATE AGREEMENT:  It is mutually agreed and understood 
that the City reserves the right to terminate this Agreement at any time, provided, however, that 
the City shall pay the Contractor on a pro rata basis for services rendered up to the time of 
cancellation.  In the event of termination, all property and documents, data, studies and reports 
purchased, prepared or related to this Agreement whether completed or in progress shall 
immediately become the property of the City and shall be delivered to the City within five (5) days 
of termination.  Notwithstanding the above, the Contractor shall not be relieved of liability to the 
City for damages sustained by the City because of any breach of this Agreement by the Contractor, 
and the City may withhold any reimbursement to the Contractor for the purpose of set-off until 
such time as the exact amount of damages due the City from the Contractor is agreed upon or 
otherwise determined. In the event the Contractor fails to strictly perform in accordance with this 
Agreement, the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Contractor. 
 
5. 
PUBLICATION AND PUBLICITY:  The Contractor may not publish results, data 
or findings arising from its performance of this Agreement in the absence of the prior written 
approval of the Human Services Advisory Council.  A copy of the publication will be furnished to 
the City. 
 
6.

of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming; and the courts of the

APPENDIX A 
 
SAFE HARBOR 
 
Mission: 
To provide a safe environment for children to: disclose abuse, benefit from 
a multidisciplinary approach to the investigation of child abuse and, 
engage in safe parental exchange and visitation.  
 
 
Program Purpose: 
To provide a safe and supportive environment for abused and neglected 
children in the City of Cheyenne, including a place where professionals 
work as a team to coordinate investigations and interventions on child abuse 
cases.

PREAMBLE 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this              day of           2023, by and between the 
City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization, hereinafter referred to as the 
“MPO”, and Ayres Associates Inc, hereinafter referred to as “Consultant.”  The City of Cheyenne 
is acting as the agent of the MPO for the contract administration and as the MPO’s Fiscal Manager 
in accordance with Contract #6111. 
 
AGREEMENT 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
Scope of Work. The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-
mentioned project, in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, 
incorporated by reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
Time of Performance. The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed in 
accordance with the Project Schedule attached hereto as Exhibit B, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof.  Any extensions of the time limit set forth in 
the Scope of Work shall be agreed upon in writing between the parties. 
 
3. 
Compensation. In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this 
Agreement, the Consultant will bill the MPO and the MPO agrees to pay Consultant a total 
cost-not-to-exceed amount of One Hundred Thirty Four Thousand, Nine Hundred Ninety 
Seven dollars and Fifty Four cents ($134,997.54), in accordance with the Fee Schedule 
provided in Exhibit C, which is attached to this Agreement, incorporated by reference, and 
expressly made a part hereof. The amounts of all such payments shall be based upon the 
Consultant’s progress, verified by the MPO, in completing the work as described in 
Exhibits A, B, and C.  Final payment shall be made following acceptance of the work by 
the MPO. Original quality reproductions of all designs, plans, reports, specifications, 
drawings, and other services rendered by the Consultant shall become the sole property of 
the MPO and shall be delivered to the MPO, immediately upon their preparation to the 
extent the MPO has satisfied its payment obligations to the Consultant. A portion of the 
payment may be held until any claims are resolved.

The Consultant agrees to comply with, and assures the compliance of its employees 
with, the information restrictions and other applicable requirements of the Privacy

the express consent of the Federal Government before the Consultant, or its 
employees operate a system of records on behalf of the Federal Government.  The 
Consultant understands that the requirements of the Privacy Act, including the civil 
and criminal penalties for violations of that Act, apply to those individuals 
involved, and that failure to comply with the terms of the privacy Act may result in 
termination of the underlying contract. 
 
B. 
The Consultant also agrees to include these requirements in each sub-contract to 
administer any system of records on behalf of the Federal Government financed in 
whole or in part with Federal assistance provided by FTA.

agency; all applicable regulations published in the Code of Federal Regulations;

Scope of Work 
 
 

Exhibit A 
West Crow Creek Greenway Plan 
Scope of Work – July 2023 
 
 
Page 1 
 
 
Cheyenne MPO West Crow Creek Greenway Plan 
Scope of Work  
7/21/2023 
 
1.0  PROJECT MANAGEMENT & ADMINISTRATION TASKS 
1.1 Administer the contract per Cheyenne MPO requirements 
1.2   Schedule & conduct a project kick-off meeting with project staff (MPO, City Engineering, Greenway 
Advisory WYDOT, and consultant team). Schedule & conduct bi-weekly progress meetings with 
project staff and prepare meeting minutes and/or summaries (assume 24 meetings). 
1.3 Prepare monthly invoices and progress reports. 
1.4 Schedule & conduct deliverable review meetings with project staff after major submittals (assume 2 
meetings). 
1.5 Schedule & conduct internal discipline-specific coordination meetings as necessary to complete the 
work. 
 
2.0  PUBLIC & STAKEHOLDER INVOLVEMENT TASKS 
2.1 Task management & internal coordination meetings 
2.2 Develop a public involvement plan in collaboration with the MPO that is uniquely tailored to the 
needs of this project to achieve public education and feedback solicitation, stakeholder consensus, 
and community support 
2.3 Coordinate with the MPO and City regarding various media project information briefings and public 
outreach materials to inform the public of the project and collect public input, including advertisement 
and active solicitation. Provide public engagement information and materials to City staff for use on 
the MPO website and other associated social media platforms.  
a. Design all digital and traditional advertising materials for the project, including surveys 
b. Produce all meeting materials and provide these materials to MPO staff at least one week 
prior to all meetings, for use at meetings and on the MPO website and other associated social 
media platforms.  
2.4 Plan, coordinate, and hold one stakeholder site walk activity and three stakeholder coordination 
meetings. Coordination meetings should be jointly held if possible. City/MPO will develop the invitee 
list for the site walk. 
2.5 Plan, coordinate, and hold two public open house meetings. Develop informational flyers & invites. 
MPO will develop mailing list and send mailings. Provide public engagement results to staff for use 
on the MPO website and other associated social media platforms. 
2.6 Develop meeting summaries for public and stakeholder meetings and submit summaries to MPO. 
2.7 Prepare and present project and report related materials at twelve Committee and Commission 
meetings and one City Council Presentation. 
 
3.0  STRUCTURES TASKS  
3.1 Review potential impacts to existing structures at proposed Greenway grade-separated crossing 
locations on the Greenway corridor (assume 3 structures) 
3.2 Provide general structural improvement recommendations for existing structures. 
3.3 Provide planning-level cost estimates for proposed structures and modifications. 
 

Exhibit A 
West Crow Creek Greenway Plan 
Scope of Work – July 2023 
 
 
Page 2 
 
4.0  ENGINEERING & CONCEPTUAL DESIGN TASKS 
4.1 Review and document existing traffic volumes and roadway conditions at proposed Greenway 
crossing locations, including roadway speed limits, traffic control devices, and pedestrian and bicycle 
facilities. 
4.2 In coordination with the MPO, develop 2045 future traffic volume projections based on the latest 
PlanCheyenne travel demand model forecasts for the area. 
4.3 Conduct a brief Pedestrian Safety & Operations Analysis for existing 2045 conditions, including: 
a. Identify and prepare a summary of vehicle, pedestrian, and bicycle concerns, constraints, and 
opportunities at each crossing location. 
b. Recommend the appropriate controls for each crossing, including signing, striping, and signal 
improvements.  
i. Submit initial recommendations to the MPO for review.  
ii. Incorporate recommendations into the Plan.   
4.4 Develop concept-level plans for the greenway corridor for up to two Greenway alignment 
alternatives, up to four at-grade crossing alternatives, and up to three grade-separated crossing 
alternatives. 
4.5 Support the alternatives analysis and concept screening matrix for scoring and selection of 
alternatives. 
4.6 Develop preliminary design plans for Greenway, at-grade crossings, and potential grade-separated 
crossings, to include: 
a. Title sheet 
b. Typical section sheets 
c. Greenway plan and profile sheets 
d. Proposed At-Grade Crossing Plans, including preliminary signal plans (as necessary) 
e. Conceptual Grade-Separated Crossing Plans (as appropriate) 
4.7 Develop concept-level estimate of probable cost for construction and Right of Way acquisitions 
4.8 Develop draft and final reports in 2 hard copies and pdf format, thoroughly addressing the following: 
a. Summary of existing conditions and relevant existing reports, plans, and documents reviewed 
b. Project Stakeholder and Public Involvement and Engagement (PI) efforts  
c. Engineering, utility, planning, environmental, and fiscal considerations & constraints 
d. Greenway and crossing alternatives evaluated 
e. Preferred alternative(s), including greenway alignment and crossing types & locations 
f. Preliminary engineering and vertical and horizontal design of Greenway and crossings, including 
typical sections, plan & profiles, traffic controls, structure layouts as appropriate, conceptual 
ROW ownerships and impacts, and conceptual utility impacts. 
g. Engineer’s Opinion of Probable Cost for Interim and Ultimate recommended improvements. 
 
The Report will be developed and submitted in the format of a NEPA clearance document to 
facilitate future clearances required by federal grant funding sources. 
Design Criteria to be followed: 
• City of Cheyenne Greenway Plan Update, Volume II, Design Guidelines and Policy Handbook 
• City of Cheyenne Zoning and Development Code 
• FHWA Manual of Uniform Traffic Control Devices (MUTCD) 
• NACTO Urban Street Design Guidelines 
• AASHTO Guide for the Development of Bicycle Facilities  
 
 

Exhibit A 
West Crow Creek Greenway Plan 
Scope of Work – July 2023 
 
 
Page 3 
 
5.0  HYDROLOGY AND HYDRAULIC ENGINEERING TASKS 
Using available terrain data, including LIDAR and other information, the Consultant shall provide a 
general hydraulic analysis of potential impacts to the Crow Creek floodplain due to the various proposed 
Greenway improvements and prepare a Drainage Memo that addresses each alternative to be included in 
the Final Report. The analysis should include recommendations for Greenway improvements that achieve 
a No-Rise condition to the extent feasible. The Drainage Memo shall be signed by a Wyoming Registered 
Professional Engineer (PE). The study related tasks are:  
5.1 Task management & internal coordination meetings 
5.2 Data Collection and Hydrology  
a. Collect and review drainage basin data  
5.3 Hydraulics  
a. Estimate the floodway impacts of initial Greenway alignment alternatives.  
b. Analyze the floodway impacts of final Greenway alignment alternatives and make floodway 
mitigation recommendations to achieve a No-Rise condition:  
c. Inform design of major underpass structures (assume 3 proposed structures):  
i. Hydraulic analysis and water surface profiles.  
ii. Determine design storm and 100-year water surface elevations.  
iii. Inform channel cross-section and determine required openings proposed of structures  
5.4 Prepare hydraulic design portions of the Final Report (the Plan) 
i. Contribute hydraulics summary of findings to insert in the Final Report 
ii. Appendix shall contain Drainage Basin Maps (existing and proposed conditions), 
Hydrology/hydraulic work sheets, and culvert & bridge calculations as appropriate.
